# DSL Observer UI — Changelog

Format (corrected 2026-08-23 — the old `[Date] [Version] Description`
line hadn't matched real practice since June and an independent review
pass correctly called that out): one entry per real change, in the
literal shape `YYYY-MM-DD verb(scope): what happened and why`. No
version numbers — this project doesn't use them. Claude Code appends one
entry per commit (occasionally bundling several small related fixes
under one entry, not one commit per fix); earlier Claude.ai-era entries
appended session summaries instead, in the same append-only style.

---

## Session: June 9, 2026 — Research & Contract Phase

**No code changes made this session.**

### What was accomplished:
- Uploaded DSL2 autosave.xml — all 13 Layer 2/3 scripts extracted and available
- Full Mudlet API education: built Parts 1 & 2 of API reference
- Built feature comparison matrix (DSL1 vs PNP vs new MyDSL)
- Built project backlog from all scattered notes
- Built DSL_CommandRef.md from actual in-game text captures
- Written Contract_DataLayer.md from actual code review
- Established three-tool workflow (Claude.ai + Claude Code + Steven)
- Confirmed git branch strategy and session rituals

### DataLayer bugs confirmed:
- 10 broken score parser patterns (wrong field names/formats)
- 2 flags parser bugs (can't detect on/off state)
- Who parser broken for hyphenated races
- Lunar, time, affects text parsers all wrong format
- Missing: no equipment section

### DSL game data confirmed:
- Prompt is 3 lines, event-driven (not per-command)
- GMCP fires after every server response
- 14 chat channels confirmed
- Moon system: 3 moons, Kien sees red+white only (neutral alignment)
- Calendar: 1 year = 13 real days 15 minutes

### Files created this session:
- `DSL_CommandRef.md` — in-game text patterns with confirmed Lua patterns
- `Contract_DataLayer.md` — DataLayer contract with all confirmed bugs
- `DSL_SessionNotes.md` — session log
- `SESSION_START.md` — this update
- `CHANGELOG.md` — this file
- `TODO.md` — actionable backlog
- `README.md` — project master index

## Session: June 14–21, 2026 — Full Layer 2/3 Phase A Contract Pass

**No code changes made this session — pure design/contract/research session.**

### Contracts written from actual extracted code:
- Contract_ThemeEngine.md — persistence, character-binding, named presets designed
- Contract_LayoutEngine.md — screenshot-corrected positions for all 21 windows
- Contract_WindowRegistry.md — MyDSL_Location rename, MyDSL_Mapper removal decided
- Contract_DataBridge.md — 6 gaps found, 3 entire sections (time/affects/score) unmapped
- Contract_RouteHelper.md — color-stripping bug found (Gap 1), routeMap dropped
- Contract_TickSource.md — 5-second warning bug confirmed (config exists, logic missing)
- Contract_TickView.md — confirmed visual warning DOES work, only audio/echo missing
- Contract_ChatWrapper.md — 5.0s forced rebuild wipes early-session chat (Gap 2)
- Contract_AffectsView.md — confirmed best-quality module, registerHandlerOnce is the template
- Contract_PortraitView.md — CharPic removal confirmed and decided
- Contract_LocationView.md — confirmed roomData() is LiveView's primary room data source
- Contract_LiveView.md — baseline only, deliberately paused for full design session

### Major architecture discovery:
- Mudlet has native UserWindow docking (docked/dockPosition/autoDock/restoreLayout)
  and native layout persistence (saveWindowLayout/loadWindowLayout with version
  numbers) that replaces most of LayoutEngine's planned custom persistence system
- Multiple windows docked to the same side auto-tab via Mudlet's Qt layer —
  confirmed by screenshot (Map/Scan/Combat tab together on the left)
- Critical caveat found: loadWindowLayout() must be called once, after all
  windows are created — calling it per-window resets every other window

### DSL1 mapper script fully audited:
- 8 confirmed DSL-specific patches on top of stock generic_mapper v2.1.8
- All center on room-description matching/cleaning for duplicate room names
- download_path disabled to protect patches from being overwritten by auto-update
- Must be carried forward to DSL2 as-is, never reinstalled fresh

### Screenshots analyzed (3 total):
- Full DSL1 layout reference — corrected all 21 window positions
- Stacked/tabbed dock confirmation — Map/Scan/Combat native tab group on left
- Tick window position — standalone, between Live and Target, Alterform slot confirmed for future

### Prompt system fully designed:
- Toggleable pretty prompt (gag 3 lines + new PromptBar overlay)
- Confirmed no extra prompt-text capture needed — alignment from score,
  day/night derived from time command (existing trigger runs it 2x/game-day)

### GMCP structure further confirmed via live captures:
- Two time-command format variants found and documented
- Affect fields (n/d/lc/m/t) cross-verified against AffectsView's correct handling
- (AR) = Arkane kingdom confirmed
- Two moons visible to Kien (red/white), black moon confirmed evil-only

### Files created/updated this session:
- 8 new Contract_*.md files (ThemeEngine, LayoutEngine, WindowRegistry,
  DataBridge, RouteHelper, TickSource, TickView, ChatWrapper, AffectsView,
  PortraitView, LocationView — final count includes earlier-session DataLayer)
- Contract_Addendum_2026-06-21.md — cross-cutting decisions superseding
  parts of LayoutEngine, WindowRegistry, RouteHelper, PortraitView contracts
- SESSION_START.md — full rewrite reflecting all 12 contracted modules
- TODO.md — full rewrite, blocking items clearly separated from design work
- CHANGELOG.md — this entry

## Session: June 21, 2026 (continued) — LiveView Design Finalized

No code changes — design and validation session.

### LiveView fully designed via iterative collaboration:
- Built interactive drag-and-drop mockups (Visualizer tool) so the user could
  physically arrange stat blocks rather than describe layouts in words
- Final layout: 12-row design, header (room/exits/identity/badges/time) +
  attribute rows paired with their combat stat (mirroring DSL's own score
  sheet pairing: STR+Hit, INT+Dam, WIS+Armor) + four bars (HP/Mana/Move/
  Improve) with graduated danger-color thresholds
- No compact/full toggle — single unified view per explicit decision
- Window is floating with restoreLayout=true (not docked), allowed to grow
  taller than the standard bottom-strip height; user resizes once

### Validated against real Mudlet/Qt constraints before finalizing:
- Confirmed Geyser gauges (front/back/text triple-label pattern), dynamic
  per-render color swapping, and percentage+pixel mixed positioning are all
  supported — consistent with patterns already used in TickView/PortraitView
- Found: Qt QLabel does NOT support CSS text-overflow:ellipsis — room name
  truncation must be done via Lua string cutting, not a stylesheet property
- Found: absolute pixel positioning breaks at different resolutions (same
  bug already flagged in MyDSL_Audit.md for old LocationView) — all LiveView
  children use percentage positions instead

### New gaps surfaced (added to TODO.md):
- DataLayer: Items count was never parsed at all — new field, not a fix
- DataBridge: needs hitroll/damroll/armor/items/posn added to DB.score
- DataBridge Gap 3 (DB.time): priority raised, now has a concrete consumer

### Files updated this session:
- Contract_LiveView.md — full rewrite, baseline superseded entirely
- SESSION_START.md, TODO.md — LiveView moved from "next" to "done"

2026-07-05 fix(combat): dodge/parry/block triggers only matched third-party phrasing ("Mob dodges Name's attack") and missed the you-as-subject/your-attack forms ("You dodge Mob's attack.", "Mob dodges your attack.") because the old regex hardcoded "dodges"/"parries"/"blocks" (3rd-person verb form only) and required a literal "'s attack"; replaced all three with DSL_PNP_Battle.lua's verbatim tested patterns (dodge/parry/block[s]? + your|Name's alternation) ported to our PCRE double-backslash convention; parseCombatAvoidLine(evader, verb, attacker) now reads the 3 capture groups directly instead of re-parsing getCurrentLine() with its own Lua patterns (sense triggers unchanged, still whole-line parsed); cross-check against PNP condition/death triggers found a confirmed-live gap (DSL-Logs show "You have some small wounds"/"You are in excellent condition"/"You look pretty hurt" -- second-person verb forms) that neither PNP nor our CONDITION_PATTERNS table handles at all -- self-condition currently never registers; not fixed here, flagged for follow-up
2026-07-05 audit: full PNP cross-check beyond evasion triggers (per Steven's request) — verified parseCombatAvoidLine cleanup (single 3-arg def, no duplicates), reworded combatDodge comment for accuracy; confirmed via DSL-Logs that self-condition uses second-person verbs ("You have some small wounds"/"You are in excellent condition"/"You look pretty hurt") vs third-person for others -- gap flagged in 2026-07-05 evasion-trigger entry above, still unfixed; searched DSL_PNP_Character.lua and DSL_PNP_Affects.lua for finishing-blow/execute mechanics -- none found, but DSL-Logs reveal "<mob> hits the ground ... DEAD." is a very common death form (188 occurrences in one session log alone) that NEITHER PNP nor our combatDead trigger (" is DEAD!!$") handles at all -- likely means most player-delivered killing blows never fire MyDSL.combat.ended; re-verified all 14 flag/proc trigger regex against DSL_PNP_Battle.lua lines 476-489 character-for-character (all 14 match) but found a deeper issue: PNP's flag handler never trusts names captured by the flag trigger itself (uses last_attacker/last_target/last_noun from the most recent damage line instead) specifically because those captures are often a WEAPON name, not the wielder -- confirmed live via DSL-Logs ("A whisper thin blade of satiny steel draws life from...", "is knocked to the ground by a grand arcanium polearm."); our procFlameBurn/procShockLightning/procVampDraw/procStun (and likely procFrostFreeze) pass that captured name straight into normalizeKey() as the attacker key, so entry.by_attacker[attackerKey] lookup silently fails and the flag is dropped every time -- not fixed here, architecture decision needed (adopt PNP's last-attacker-tracking model); staleness check found Contract_ScanView.md and Contract_TargetView.md both still show pre-fix PCRE code samples (scanDir %a+%./considerLine2 %.%.%. ) even though live code was already corrected 2026-07-03 -- Contract_GroupView.md is current; added "read PNP source directly" caveat to Contract_CombatWindow.md and MyDSL_PNP_Reference.md; docs/claude_export_2026-07-05/ created with state report + current file copies for Steven to upload to Claude.ai project

DEAD." alongside "<mob> is DEAD!!" -- confirmed via a full session log with 188 kills of this form and zero "is DEAD!!"; snapshotFight() already no-ops on an already-cleared target so no double-snapshot risk if both fire; (2) parseCombatProcLine now checks isKnownCombatant(attackerKey) (you/group member) -- weapon-flag procs whose "attacker" capture is actually a weapon name (confirmed: "A grand arcanium hoopak draws life from Rylae.", "is knocked to the ground by a runehammer.") now get their own by_attacker[weaponKey]["(proc)"] pseudo-row instead of being silently dropped; deliberate simplification, not full wielder resolution -- documented in Contract_CombatWindow.md; (3) procFrostFreeze/procVampDraw/procStun char classes now include " (double-quote) and strip it via new stripQuotes() helper before normalizeKey -- fixes quoted weapon names like "Nadrik's Honor" breaking the match; all three verified with luajit against real captured text. 2026-07-05 fix: made the new per-window debug logs (MyDSL.logWindow, added this session) character-bound -- MyDSL/logs/<category>/<CharName>/<date>.log instead of a flat shared file per category, using MyDSL.Char() (falls back to "Unknown") + the same safeFileName() sanitizer as AffectsView. 2026-07-05 fix(combat): round-flush handler was wired to the wrong event -- confirmed root cause of Steven's live-test report ("still only seeing the combat report not the actual combat in combatwindow") after fighting on Vrokt; MyDSL/logs/combat/Vrokt/2026-07-05.log showed only the Fight summary block, zero round-by-round lines, proving the earlier show_damage_by_me/show_damage_to_me/echo_to_main removal was necessary but not sufficient. Root cause: MyDSL._handlers.combatRoundFlush listened on "MyDSL.time.updated" (fires only when the player types "time") instead of "MyDSL.char.updated" (raised on every gmcp.char_data packet -- the actual once-per-round vitals-refresh signal); round_data accumulated correctly the whole fight but never got flushed to CV.render() until either a manual "time" or a kill via the separate snapshotFight() path, which is exactly why fight summaries worked while the live round log never appeared. Fixed by switching the event name.

2026-07-05 feat: wire up "Players near you:" -> MyDSL_PlayersNear routing + gag -- Steven asked "where does that stand?"; investigation found MyDSL.Route.players() already existed fully working in MyDSL_RouteHelper.lua but was never called from anywhere, and MyDSL_PlayersNear was a registered window with nothing ever routing into it (the existing scan catch-all only used the "Players near you:" line as an end-of-scan-block signal, never gagged or routed it). Added MyDSL.beginPlayersNear()/endPlayersNear() in DataLayer Section 9o.2 (mirrors beginScan/endScan exactly) plus a permanent "^Players near you:$" trigger; captures the header + each "<Name><padding><Room>" line (confirmed exact shape from log/) via Route.players(nil) (appendBuffer, preserves original colors) and gags each from main console, ending on the blank line. Contract_ScanView.md corrected (previously claimed the opposite). Note: triggered by Steven's own external "autowhere" alias (runs `where` every ~20s), not part of this project.
2026-07-05 fix: who-list parser (parseWhoLine) was silently corrupting kingdom/name fields -- first finding from the new systematic bottom-up integrity audit (per Steven's request to re-verify the project layer by layer against the reuse-PNP/EMCO mandate, starting at DataLayer). Cross-checked against DSL_PNP_People.lua's tested regex and DSL_CommandRef.md's own documented format, then confirmed live in log/: clan/org codes are in (parens) -- (NT), (VR), (Abaddon) -- not [brackets] as the old code assumed; brackets are for kingdom names only. Old entry.clan was therefore always nil, and leftover parenthetical text like (WANTED)/(VR) shifted every following word by one position, corrupting kingdom/name for any WANTED- or clan-tagged who-list entry. Rewritten to extract every ()/[] group in order and classify status tags (WANTED/Hostile/AFK -- confirmed live in bare/bracket/paren forms, DSL isn't consistent) vs org/kingdom text; verified against 5 real log examples. Dropped an unconfirmed "quiet" field never found in log/ or DSL_CommandRef.md. Also confirmed (separately) that our GMCP-first vitals approach is correct as-is, not a reinvention gap -- PNP's Statusbar module is text/prompt-based only because it predates/works around limited GMCP.

2026-07-06 feat(combat): rewrote the display engine to port PNP's actual logic verbatim, replacing our from-scratch condensed-table format -- per Steven's direct request ("i would like to reverse our process... use pnp source and plug in what we want to do... make it work like pnp, then discuss the additions"). Traced DSL_PNP_Battle.lua's handle_damage/handle_evasion/handle_condition/handle_flag/calc_dam_verb/output_damage/battle_format functions in full and ported the ~50% that's pure logic (dam_info severity/decoration table, battle_format token substitution, per-swing sentence construction, round-summary aggregation) close to verbatim, replacing the ~50% that's PNP-infrastructure-specific (raw windowManager/sidebar/toggle system, PNP's own event bus) with our existing Geyser/event-name/tempRegexTrigger equivalents. Verified the ported format logic byte-for-byte against real confirmed combat examples via a standalone test script before considering this done (caught and fixed one bug this way -- an extra space accidentally inserted between the decorated verb and target name). MyDSL_CombatView.lua: Combat window is now a running per-swing log (CV.appendSwing(), never cleared) instead of a per-round condensed redraw, matching PNP's battle_console exactly; CV.config now mirrors PNP's actual defaults table; added "mydsl combat mode raw|condensed|gag" alias implementing the 3-way toggle directly on PNP's own gag_combat/summarize_damage flags. MyDSL_DataLayer.lua: round-aggregate summaries now go to main console gated by summarize_damage (independent of gag_combat, confirmed this matches PNP's actual output_damage() exactly); weapon-flag proc attribution now uses PNP's real last_attacker/last_target/last_noun technique instead of the pseudo-attacker-row workaround from the previous pass -- isKnownCombatant()/stripQuotes() removed as dead code. Contract_CombatWindow.md flagged with a staleness banner (full rewrite pass still needed, not done this round). Needs live-test confirmation -- none of this exercised against a real fight yet. Fixed 8 dangling references to the deleted files across DSL_CommandRef.md, MyDSL_MudletAPIReference.md, and two Lua source comments (MyDSL_DataLayer.lua, MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua), pointing each at the actual .lua source instead.
2026-07-06 audit: log-corpus regression test extended project-wide -- extended the trigger-pattern extractor (previously combat-only, 53 patterns) to parse every tempRegexTrigger/tempTrigger call across every MyDSL_*.lua module, including [[long-bracket]]/[=[...]=] Lua string literals and MyDSL_ChatTriggers.lua's route(tabName, pattern) helper indirection, then ran all 82 extracted patterns against the full 996,034-line log corpus. Confirmed real bugs (all added to TODO.md with detail): (1) MyDSL_ChatTriggers.lua channel routing is almost entirely non-functional -- of ~20 route() patterns only auctions: matches real DSL text; DSL formats gossip/shout/tell/pray/whisper as "Name verb 'message'" (quote-based), not "Name verb:" (colon-based); clan-says/kingdom-says are also the wrong verb (real: "X clan gossips '...'"); QUEST:/[OOC]/[OCLAN]/[OKING] don't match anything (only [OMB] found in corpus); (2) MyDSL._triggers.weather's pattern ^[A-Z][^.]+\.$ matched 131,876 of 996,034 lines (13%) including login-screen prompts, not just weather; (3) TargetView's considerLine1/2 confirmed still wrong (invented text, not real DSL "X looks like an easy kill." format, and not in PNP source either); (4) reconfirmed the combat/proc bracket-prefix zero-match findings from the prior combat-only pass. Investigated Steven's caveat that logs are heavily modified before trusting any of this: searched all of DSL2+PNP for scripts that insert the [51]/[ Coliseum Wall ] text; found nothing, then pulled raw unprocessed HTML directly and confirmed DSL itself sends "[ Wall Name ] Name verb 'message'" -- genuine server text, not client-added, for the room-tag prefix. Steven then corrected the level-tag half of this: PNP files/DSL_PNP_Highlighter.lua lines 261-264 (replaceNames()) DOES construct "[" .. info.level .. "] " .. name and inject it in place via replace()+cinsertText() for recognized clan members when its show_clanner_level config is on -- client-side decoration, not server text as originally concluded. Whether this actually corrupts what DataLayer's own triggers see live (vs. only appearing in saved logs) depends on trigger-priority ordering between Highlighter's trigger and ours, which is unresolved -- neither sets an explicit priority today.
2026-07-06 feat: added MyDSL_RawCapture.lua -- a new diagnostic-only module, one read-only catch-all trigger (pattern ".", priority 100, no deleteLine()/replace()) that logs every line to MyDSL/logs/rawcapture/<CharName>/<date>.log untouched by any other trigger, specifically to resolve the Highlighter-priority ambiguity above empirically. Off by default (toggle: "mydsl rawlog on"/"mydsl rawlog off") since it logs literally every line of the session. Needs Steven to place its dofile() first in Mudlet's Script editor load order (GUI-configured, not doable from here) and needs live confirmation that priority 100 actually beats Highlighter's trigger -- flagged in TODO.md, not yet tested in-game.

Downgraded the procStun/procFrostFreeze/procShockLightning finding in TODO.md from "confirmed real bug" back to "unconfirmed, low priority" -- the mechanism is real and worth keeping documented, but isn't actually occurring in DSL2's own current play. Conclusion for Steven: recent/future DSL2 logs are confirmed to be the clean corpus to test against, not just assumed.
2026-07-06 audit: full re-run of the trigger/alias/keybinding regression test against a proper DSL2-era-only corpus (built from the 247 dated session logs June 6 2026 onward, excluding the 42 EMCO per-tab export files which are post-routing output, not raw server text -- 328,643 lines total). Confirmed complete extraction coverage this time (82/82 real regex triggers across every MyDSL_*.lua file that registers any). Findings: (1) MyDSL_ChatTriggers.lua's confirmed-working count is 0 of ~20 route() patterns, not 1/20 as first reported -- the "auctions:" pattern that looked like the one working case had all 405 of its matches from pre-DSL2 data, 0 in the clean corpus; (2) three previously "confirmed working" proc/affect triggers (procFlameSear, procHolyWrath, procPoisonTick) plus AffectsView's Song: format turned out to have zero DSL2-era occurrences too (their matches -- 98/31/22/25 -- were entirely pre-DSL2), downgraded to unconfirmed alongside their siblings; the "sibling procs prove the mechanism works" reasoning from the original combat audit no longer holds since the cited siblings themselves lack DSL2-era confirmation. Both added to TODO.md as concrete, user-requested gaps.
2026-07-06 fix(critical): found via real-code emulation (not regex-testing) that MyDSL_DataLayer.lua would crash on a genuinely fresh Mudlet start -- built a minimal Mudlet-API mock (tempRegexTrigger/tempAlias/cecho/Geyser stubs/etc, in scratch, not committed) specifically so the real file could be dofile()'d into a fresh Lua state exactly like Mudlet's first-ever load, instead of only checking whether patterns match text. First attempt crashed immediately: "attempt to index field '_aliases' (a nil value)" -- MyDSL._aliases was referenced by the log-toggle/help aliases (line ~86) before ever being initialized as a table (that init lived at line 205, added 2026-07-05, well after first use). This would have aborted the rest of the file's load on a true fresh start, meaning every trigger registered after that point -- all of combat, weather, chat, everything -- would silently never exist. Only masked in practice because Mudlet's Lua state persists across a same-session "reload this script," so MyDSL._aliases already existed from a prior load and the bug never surfaced during normal iterative editing. Fixed by moving _triggers/_aliases initialization to Section 1 immediately after the MyDSL = MyDSL or {} namespace guard; removed the now-redundant later re-initialization. Verified: file loads clean in a fresh Lua state via the mock harness. Needs Steven to confirm with an actual full Mudlet restart (not a script reload) before closing, since that's the only scenario that reproduces the original crash.

2026-07-07 fix(MyDSL_DataLayer.lua logWindow()): Steven asked to sample today's per-window logs to check how logging turned out -- found GroupView's and TargetView's logs came out fragmented, one word/segment per line ("[Mob]", "A throughbred stalli" on its own line, " 100%hp" on another, etc.) instead of one coherent row per group member. Root cause: those two views build a single visual row from several separate decho/dechoLink calls (class tag, name, hp%, mana%, mv%, action buttons, then a final lone "\n" call), and logWindow() used to write one full log line per CALL regardless, unconditionally appending "\n". Combat/RightHere/History never hit this since those modules already pass one complete line per call. Fixed generally at the shared logWindow() level instead of patching each view: buffers per category now, only flushes a line when a "\n" actually appears in the accumulated text, so multi-call rows reconstruct as one line with one timestamp while single-call callers behave exactly as before. Verified via the emulation harness: GroupView's exact fragmented-call pattern now produces one coherent log line per row; Combat-style single-call lines unaffected. (Separately noted, not a bug: the "A throughbred stalli" truncation itself is GroupView's intentional 20-char name limit, `m.name:sub(1, 20)`, just cutting this particular 22-char name mid-word -- cosmetic, low priority, not fixed this pass.)
2026-07-07 fix(critical regression, AffectsView): Steven's live testing found affects counting down in ~2-3 real seconds instead of ~40 -- root cause: "MyDSL.Tick.Updated" does not mean "one real game tick happened", MyDSL_TickSource.lua's T.publish() raises it unconditionally from every caller including T.updateTimer()'s own ~0.25s internal progress-bar loop, not just the real once-per-tick function, with no reason string passed through to distinguish them. Fixed A.onTickUpdated() to guard on MyDSL.DB.tick.ticks actually changing (an integer only the real per-tick function increments) before decrementing, instead of decrementing on every event firing. Verified via the emulation harness: 20 simulated fast-pulse firings with no real tick change now cause zero decrements; each actual tick-count change causes exactly one.
2026-07-07 fix(MyDSL_CharacterAssist.lua): fixed the blind/no-light/can-see vision check using real data instead of a guess, after Steven did live in-game blind testing and captured a real GMCP dump ("lua display(gmcp)" while actually blinded, plus finding the native "Gag promt line1"/"Gag promt line 2" triggers in current/autosave.xml that confirmed DSL sends TWO distinct prompt lines, one of which -- the "[HP|M|MV] [stance | alignment | language | flags]" one -- DSL2 currently gags unconditionally with zero parsing). The GMCP dump confirmed gmcp.room_data.room becomes the literal string "darkness" -- exactly the value PNP's own text-prompt parsing checked -- and that MyDSL_DataLayer.lua's gmcp.room_data handler already stores this directly as MyDSL.State.room.name, no new trigger needed. Switched checkVision() from the earlier unverified MyDSL.State.room.sector substring guess to checking MyDSL.State.room.name == "darkness" directly. Also confirmed simpler exact-text alternatives in the same session ("You are blinded!"/"You can see again.") though not needed as primary. Re-verified via the emulation harness across all three vision states with real room-name transitions.
2026-07-07 audit: investigated the "prompt gag trigger 2" Steven mentioned -- found current/autosave.xml has TWO native prompt-gag triggers, not one: "Gag promt line1" (pattern "^\[\d+/\d+HP \| \d+/\d+M \| \d+/\d+MV \] \[ .* \| .* \| .* \| .* \]$") and "Gag promt line 2" (the "==-...::[room]::[exits]-==" one MyDSL_DataLayer.lua's parsePromptLine already handles). Line1 is currently gagged with zero parsing anywhere in MyDSL -- pulled real examples from the corpus and confirmed its 4 pipe-separated fields are: combat stance (Offensive/Normal/Defensive), alignment (neutral/evil), current speaking language (Common/Dragon/Yinnish -- directly explains the "(Language)" tags on chat lines found earlier this session), and a parenthetical status-flags slot (confirmed "(Flying)" appears there; never saw "(Blind)" in this specific corpus but the room-name-based check above makes that moot). This is a real, currently-unused data source -- not acted on yet, noted for a future pass since it wasn't needed for the immediate vision-check fix.
2026-07-07 decision: closed the .gag.lua discussion per Steven -- not built. The countdown fix (below) made it moot rather than the original philosophy objection deciding it: durations no longer need any server re-query to stay accurate now that they decrement locally per real game tick, so .gag.lua's entire reason for existing doesn't apply to DSL2 regardless of whether the auto-refresh approach would have been acceptable on its own terms.
2026-07-07 feat(Phase D): new MyDSL_CharacterAssist.lua, ported from DSL_PNP_Character.disarm/spellup/standup.lua after Steven reviewed and explicitly approved each feature (unlike every other cannibalization phase, this one does send real game commands): rearm (both the auto-trigger on any of 4 weapon-disarm/2 shield-disarm forms, and a manual "rearm" alias, matching PNP's own both-together design), standup (auto ~+stand on knockdown, no manual alias, matching PNP), spellup (setspell/bless/fireproof/stop spellup/resume spellup/ignore, full per-item cast/remove/re-equip loop with a 20s failsafe). Built useItem(slot, command, location) -- PNP's own command-sending primitive -- in this module specifically, not in MyDSL_DataLayer.lua which stays passive-only; reads MyDSL.State.equipment.slots (Phase E) without ever writing it. Derives item keywords by stripping filler words from the item name rather than porting PNP's "set keyword" manual-override alias (v1 auto-derive only). One unconfirmed piece: the blind/no-light/can-see vision check uses MyDSL.State.room.sector as a best-effort "dark" substring proxy since DSL2 has no equivalent to PNP's text-prompt room field it originally checked -- couldn't verify against real data, GMCP is never in text logs, flagged in-file. Verified via the emulation harness with real DataLayer-built equipment state: rearm (combat+full), rearm-shield, standup, the worn-duplicate "2.keyword" disambiguation, and a full "fireproof all" run (start -> per-item advance -> completion) all produce exactly the right command sequences, zero errors. Deliberately not built: DSL_PNP_Affects.gag.lua's auto-refresh -- reopened for discussion per Steven rather than staying excluded on my own unilateral call; noted that GMCP's affect_data only fires reactively per existing docs, and that the specific symptom motivating the reopen (frozen timers) turned out to have a separate root cause needing no server command at all.
2026-07-07 fix(AffectsView): fixed the affects-timer countdown per Steven's report that timers "didn't seem to work correctly." Found two real bugs: entry.duration was set once by GMCP and never decremented locally, so it stayed frozen between add/remove events instead of counting down; and the "time"/"both" timer-mode display looked up MyDSL.DB.timers.affects[name].text, a table nothing ever populated, so it silently always fell back to cycles-only. Checked whether GMCP itself already pushes fresh data periodically (also relevant to the .gag.lua question) -- confirmed via docs/DSL_CommandRef.md's existing note that gmcp.affect_data only fires reactively (typing "affects", or a spell start/end), not as a periodic push. Fixed with a purely local, zero-server-command approach instead, matching PNP's own DSL_PNP_Affects.bar.lua decrement_affects() (hooked to its onTick there): new A.onTickUpdated() decrements every active affect's duration by 1 on "MyDSL.Tick.Updated" (fires once per real game tick), while GMCP add/remove events stay authoritative and always overwrite it on any real change. Real-time minutes/seconds now computed directly from MyDSL.DB.tick.average (same field MoonWeather's clock already uses for this) plus MyDSL.DB.tick.remaining for smooth between-tick counting. Verified via the emulation harness: real affect ticked 10->7 over 3 simulated ticks, zero errors, permanent-duration affects correctly skip real-time conversion.
2026-07-07 feat(Phase C): ported Roller as new MyDSL_Roller.lua. Read the native "roller" trigger in current/autosave.xml in full -- it's a character-creation stat-reroll auto-rejecter (sums Str/Int/Wis/Dex/Con, auto-sends "n" below a hardcoded goal of 241, pauses for manual review at/above goal, never auto-accepts), not a combat dice-display as the vague open TODO item implied. DSL_PNP_Roller.lua does the same core thing plus real value-add the native version lacks: adjustable goal, running min/max/avg/stdev stats across all rolls, reset -- ported that near-verbatim, reusing PNP's own set goal/roll stats/reset roll command vocabulary, keeping the native trigger's already-tuned 241 goal as the default over PNP's generic 230. PNP's own trigger pattern (no brackets, double space) and the native one (brackets, single space) disagree and neither could be verified against any real log (character creation is one-time, not captured in available logs) -- made the pattern tolerant of both instead of guessing one over the other. Verified via the real-code emulation harness: both format variants match, goal-boundary logic and all three commands run error-free.
2026-07-07 audit: rescanned all logs/screenshots/notes for new gameplay activity. One log "error" hit turned out to be a false positive (Mudlet's normal "Socket got disconnected" message after Steven typed quit and reconnected, not a Lua bug) -- useful evidence for his reload-safety question, answered directly rather than added to TODO (see notes_utf8.txt: a simple character quit/reconnect is safe and doesn't reset Mudlet's Lua state, unlike a full application restart -- most MyDSL state persisting across it, like tracked spells and window layout, is intentional, not a bug). Investigated "affects window treating skills like stealth and riot as spells" with real data: riot is confirmed genuinely a spell (real "Spell: riot ... cycles" text, correctly tracked), but stealth is confirmed a %-based skill (via the skills listing) with no "Spell: stealth" line anywhere in tonight's logs -- likely can never register as active via the affects-duration mechanism, so it sits permanently in AffectsView's "missing" list. Added both findings to docs/TODO.md, split cleanly since only one is actually a mismatch. Also logged a new TargetView design request (alternate combat-opener command, e.g. waylay instead of murder, for characters like Vrokt) as a scoped-but-not-built design idea.
2026-07-07 feat/re-scope: Phase F (Highlighter+DslColor) fully re-scoped after reading every source involved -- read DSL_PNP_Highlighter.lua/.custom.lua (445 lines), DSL_PNP_People.lua (672 lines), colors.xml (4904 lines/95 triggers), and the live native DslColors_Core_v1_0 script (3220 lines) in full. Found colors.xml is not currently loaded (zero overlap with current/autosave.xml's live trigger tree) and calls functions that don't exist in the live engine -- a stale pre-v1.0 snapshot, nothing to merge from it. Found the live DslColors_Core_v1_0 is already a complete superset of PNP's Highlighter+People combined: a real dslcolor command surface (friend/enemy/neutral, war/peace, show, list, census, team, killed/killedby/downed/downedby + combat notes, name override, term, title, palette, ignore/save/reload/test) backed by a persistent DSL_COLOR_DB with per-person records (race/class/kingdom/clan/robe/dragon/title/team/first_seen/last_seen/room/crafts/combat history) and friend-enemy/war-peace relation tracking at both person and org level. Porting PNP's Highlighter/People now would just build redundant, inferior duplicate functionality -- moved both files to explicitly-out-of-scope alongside DSL_PNP_Statusbar.*. Built and syntax-checked the first real integration instead of just scoping: MyDSL_TargetView.lua now shows a [Friend]/[Enemy] tag next to the current target's name, read live (read-only, pcall-guarded) from DSL_COLOR_DB.relations.people -- setting relations still goes through the real "dslcolor friend/enemy <name>" command, no duplicate alias added. Updated docs/TODO.md's Phase F entry, the DslColor design-idea entry, and the out-of-scope list to match.
2026-07-07 audit: found and confirmed the itemstat A/B comparison Steven asked about, directly in the main session log. He ran eq/in with the native itemstat triggers on, then disabled them and ran eq/in again -- same items, and every single stat suffix vanished (e.g. "a ring of wizardry -[35] 30M,-1S" became plain "a ring of wizardry"). Confirms the itemstat system isn't only "type an item name as a command" -- it also live-decorates eq/in output lines with "-[level] stat,..." suffixes, entirely client-side. Surfaces a real dependency: MyDSL_DataLayer.lua's Section 9r equipment parser captures that suffix as unparsed text and only works today because the native itemstat trigger is what puts it there -- if itemstats get disabled permanently before Layer 4 ships a replacement, MyDSL.State.equipment would silently lose all stat data. Documented in docs/TODO.md's existing itemstats-interference item. Also reviewed 6 new screenshots (18:15-19:07) -- confirmed CreatureReference/lore working live against multiple real mobs (throughbred stallion, wild bear, gnome architect/factory worker), a third consider-tier ("The perfect match!") not previously recorded, and real affects-countdown/prompt-line-1 data matching what the logs already showed; no new bugs found in the screenshots themselves.
2026-07-07 audit: investigated Steven's new note ("im not seeing the debufs i cast on the target, like weaken and slow?"). Traced tonight's logs around his own "c weaken"/"c slow" casts and confirmed two real, distinct success messages: weaken -> "<target> looks tired and weak." (checked against the existing 7-phrase HP-condition ladder at MyDSL_DataLayer.lua:1723, not a match, genuinely separate), slow -> "<target> starts to move in slow motion." Per an in-game help newbie lookup found in an earlier log, these are "Maladictions" that persist until cured/cancelled rather than on a short fixed timer -- no MyDSL code currently tracks or displays either. Added as a new TargetView design item (enemy-side counterpart to AffectsView's player-side buff tracking), not scoped yet -- needs a wider catalog of maladiction spells and their end-text before building.
2026-07-07 audit: rescanned all logs for new info since the last pass. Found a real help-file lookup Steven did mid-session for the healing spell group -- confirmed the actual power-ordered hierarchy (cure light -> refresh -> cure serious -> cure critical -> heal -> mass healing, syntax cast '<name>' <character>, classes ASSASSIN/BATTLERAGER/CLERIC/CRUSADER/DRAGON/GIANT/DRUID/PALADIN/PRIEST/SHADOWKNIGHT/WARRIOR) -- folded into the GroupView multi-heal-tier design gap in TODO.md. Also found Steven's own lua cecho() marker in the log pinpointing the order-all-kill failure moment: surrounding text shows the game answered with a plain "Ok." and no attack, narrowing the bug toward a follower-targeting/state issue rather than a broken alias pattern (not confirmed). No new errors in any of today's three session logs; combat/chat still actively logging live; group/righthere/target/scan logs unchanged since the last scan (still need re-enabling this session if wanted).
2026-07-07 audit: folded Steven's live-test notes (notes_utf8.txt "claude notes" section) into docs/TODO.md. GroupView heal-on-Mob-rows resolved as not a bug -- Kien (has heal skill) healing a bear via the Group window worked live; the earlier failure was Tibbins, who simply has no heal skill at all. New design gap surfaced by the same test: more than one heal-tier skill exists and the Group window's [Heal] button needs to use whichever one the character actually has, not a hardcoded command. New items added: TargetView shows an attack-capable "kill" option on your own group members/followers (real safety issue, not cosmetic); native (oac) order-all-kill failed twice in live testing (first elf in combat, then a greaser from the target window); CombatView/History font sizes requested (8/7, should route through the existing per-window font-persistence pattern); action-button blue color contrast flagged as a real accessibility problem (unreadable without hovering for the tooltip); a design direction for more IC/human-speak command vocabulary; and the Skills/Spells-to-Combat idea clarified with three specifics (echo to main too, Combat should get "everything" like PNP's did, remove raw attack-spam in favor of real actions taken).
2026-07-07 audit: confirmed both native prompt gag triggers ("Gag promt line1"/"Gag promt line 2", both isActive="no" under MyDSL_GameplayTriggers) have served their verification purpose and can be re-enabled. Extracted real "==-...::...::...==" prompt-line text from fresh post-reload logs (e.g. "==-Dawn - 6:00am :: [darkness] :: [ESW]-=="), hand-traced parsePromptLine()'s pattern against it and confirmed it correctly captures the period name; the room-name and exits brackets on that line duplicate GMCP fields already captured elsewhere. Prompt Line 1's bracket format (stance/alignment/language/flags) was already confirmed from corpus data earlier and is likely fully redundant with GMCP too -- no parser built, none currently planned. Updated docs/TODO.md's "Parse Prompt Line 1" entry to record both as safe-to-regag rather than open questions.
2026-07-07 correction: retracted the "confirmed duplicate native trigger group" finding from the 2026-07-07 full inventory. That finding claimed "Toasts/History Captures" and "Trumpet Sounds and History" were two trigger groups with byte-for-byte identical children routing chat channels (including clan gossip), named as the likely root cause of the open "clan gossip duplicates" bug. Found wrong while trying to write exact GUI steps for Steven to disable one, by properly parsing current/autosave.xml with xml.etree instead of trusting the prior text-scan summary: both are single leaf Triggers (not groups) under a "Notifications" folder, with completely different scripts/regexes -- one matches PvP kill-toast phrases, the other kingdom-join/retirement/achievement announcements, neither matches gossip/chat text. Also confirmed the native "Chat Routing" group (which does contain three distinct, non-duplicate gossip triggers) is itself isActive="no" (dormant), ruling it out too. Nothing was disabled based on the wrong claim. "Clan gossip duplicates" reopened as fully unsolved -- needs live repro, not another guess. Corrected all three places in TODO.md that repeated this claim.
2026-07-07 fix(chat command-surface): closed the `emco <verb>` TODO item after tracing the actual object graph instead of implementing the previously-drafted fix blind -- that draft would have broken the live chat window. Confirmed emco show/hide operate on demonnic.container (the original native Adjustable.Container, permanently hidden by MyDSL_ChatWrapper.lua's C.hideOldPrebuilt() since MyDSL replaced it with its own WindowRegistry-integrated C.window), so mydsl chat show/hide are not a duplication and were left alone. Confirmed emco font/fontSize/blink/blankLine/timestamp read demonnic.chat fresh per-call, which MyDSL reassigns to its own live object -- so emco fontSize <n> and mydsl chat font <n> really do call the same ch:setFontSize() on the same object, a real but low-stakes duplication (left as-is since mydsl chat font also persists to MyDSL's own settings file). Added code comments at MyDSL_ChatWrapper.lua's C.setFont() and the show/hide alias registrations recording this so it isn't miscorrected again. Also traced (not fully verified) that emco save/load use a module-level upvalue only refreshed by a function MyDSL never calls, so they likely act on the stale original object rather than the live chat -- flagged, not asserted as confirmed. Updated CLAUDE.md, MyDSL_MudletAPIReference.md, and TODO.md to match.
2026-07-07 audit: resolved the last open EMCO-internals uncertainty in MyDSL_MudletAPIReference_Part2.md Section 19. Confirmed EMCOChat/emco.lua is byte-for-byte identical (same md5) to the genuine upstream ~/Downloads/EMCO-2.9.0.zip's src/resources/emco.lua -- unmodified real source, not a fork. Read EMCO:append()/EMCO:xEcho() directly (lines 1660/1781): append(tabName, excludeAll) calls self:xEcho(tabName, nil, 'a', excludeAll), and xEcho itself internally does getCurrentLine() gag-check -> selectCurrentLine() -> copy() -> deselect() -> resetFormat() -> console:appendBuffer(). Both previously-discussed descriptions were correct, just describing different call frames of the same chain -- no remaining uncertainty on this point.
2026-07-07 correction: re-corrected the EMCO `emco show/hide/font` claim a second time -- the 2026-07-06 "fix" (which said no such alias exists) was itself wrong. Root cause: that check read only ~800 chars past the `<name>emco</name>` tag in current/autosave.xml and never reached the `<regex>` tag, which sits after a long multi-branch `<script>` body -- so it concluded "not found" from an incomplete read, not a real absence. Re-checked properly (reading the full `<Alias>` block) and confirmed the bare `emco` alias is isActive="yes" with regex `^emco (save|load|font|fontSize|blink|blankLine|timestamp|show|hide)(?: (.+))?$`, matching ~/Downloads/EMCO-2.9.0.zip's own aliases.json exactly. So emco show/hide/font/fontSize/blink/blankLine/timestamp/save/load are all real, live commands, and MyDSL_ChatWrapper.lua:622-625 (mydsl chat show/hide/font) does genuinely duplicate them -- reopened as a real command-surface bug in TODO.md. Corrected CLAUDE.md, MyDSL_MudletAPIReference.md, and TODO.md to match, flagging the mistake explicitly in each rather than silently re-editing.
2026-07-07 fix(Phase A, third pass): per Steven, voice types and languages both affect real chat text and weren't handled generally by the second pass's narrow "(?: \([^)]+\))?" language-tag group. DSL_Helpfiles/voicetype.txt confirms 21 distinct voice types (Soft, Raspy, Low toned, Growlingly, Husky, ...) with irregular phrasing ("says softly"/"says in a raspy voice"/"says in a low toned manner" -- no single template), and real corpus data shows a "(to Name)" target tag can combine with a "(Language)" tag in either order (e.g. "Ariaenys says in a musical tone (to You) (Dragon) 'message'"). Rather than enumerate all 21 phrasings, replaced the narrow group everywhere with a general "[^']*'" zone (anything up to the opening quote) -- correctly covers any combination of voice-phrase/target-tag/language-tag/none without needing each one's exact wording. Verified via the clean corpus: counts stayed sane (Local says 2,759 -> 2,959, not unbounded) and all 139 real voice-type "says" lines are now covered (0 missed, some were missed by the narrower group before). Voice-type modifiers only appear on "says" in the corpus so far, applied the general form everywhere anyway as free future-proofing.
2026-07-06 fix(Phase A): rewrote MyDSL_ChatTriggers.lua a second time using the native-XML-sourced ground truth above -- corrects several things the first same-day rewrite got wrong from log-corpus guessing alone: clan gossip's real verb is "clan gossips" (two words, not the guessed "cgossips"), group chat is "tells the group" (not "group-says"), OOC is "Name OOC: 'message'" (not a bracket tag), congratulate's verb is "grats" (not "congratulates"), Kingdom is a literal "Kingdom: '" prefix (not a verb), plus three previously-unknown channels (Radios, Bloodbath, Quest). Also fixed a real bug in the first rewrite: its yell/shout patterns required the third-person -s form even for "You", so "You yell '...'"/"You shout '...'" (first-person) never matched -- native XML confirms both forms are real. Corrected several wrong tab-destination assignments (auctions/grats/radios/ask/newbie/bloodbath/quest all route to OOC, not City). Kept the confirmed-from-logs optional (Language) tag group on every pattern since that's independently verified real and better than the native reference. Every pattern re-verified against the clean corpus. Corrected CLAUDE.md's wrong emco show/hide/font claim and the matching TODO.md command-surface entry.
2026-07-06 audit: extended the real-code emulation to ChatTriggers and the render/display layer (CombatView/GroupView/ScanView/TargetView), completing the sweep. Loaded the real EMCOChat/emco.lua library into the mock (added string.trim/table.contains/deepcopy etc as needed) -- it loads clean, but a full EMCO:new() construction needs deep internal layout state (tabHeight, Geyser.HBox chains) that's really EMCO's own internals, not DSL2 code, so descoped that part as low-value. Instead verified what actually matters: fired each of the 6 confirmed-real ChatTriggers patterns' real handler against a real matched corpus line with a lightweight recording demonnic.chat stub -- all 6 correctly called append() with the right destination tab (Tells/City/Local), zero errors (first attempt fired every handler regardless of match since the mock's tempRegexTrigger doesn't gate on pattern -- test-harness gap, fixed by invoking only the specific handler Python had already confirmed matches each line). Then ran a full real fight sequence through the actual DataLayer parse functions in registration order (damage/avoid/condition/death, all real confirmed text) with raiseEvent upgraded to record instead of no-op -- confirmed the real previously-untestable local snapshotFight() fires via "MyDSL.combat.ended" with a real snapshot, fed that into the real CV.renderSummary() -- clean. Also verified CV.renderRage/CV.appendSwing (incl. nil/no-arg edge cases) and GroupView.render()/ScanView.render()/ScanView.renderRightHere()/TargetView.render(), each built from real state via their own real begin/parse/end functions -- all clean. One test mistake caught along the way (not a real bug): MyDSL.Target (data namespace) is not MyDSL.TargetView (render namespace) -- called the wrong one first, got a nil-value error, corrected. This closes out the real-code-emulation effort for DataLayer's parsing layer, ChatTriggers routing, and the four view modules' render layer -- all confirmed error-free against real captured data. AffectsView/MoonWeather/CreatureReference render layers not covered this pass -- not flagged as broken, just not yet exercised.
2026-07-06 audit: completed the real-capture parse-function stress test for MyDSL_DataLayer.lua (resumed after the daily-limit break, using the same scratch mock/harness). Fed real matched captures/lines from the clean 328,643-line corpus directly into every remaining parse function (bypassing trigger dispatch): parseCombatAvoidLine (273 dodge/parry/block matches plus the sense-trigger single-arg call shape), parseCombatConditionLine (628), parseCombatDeathLine (94), parseWhoLine (23,056 broad-net bracket-shaped candidates), and all 14 of parseScoreLine/parseFlagsLine/parseTimeLine/parsePromptLine/parseWeatherLine/parseWhokLine/parseWhocLine/parseGroupLine/parseUnreadLine/parseInvLine/parseMapLine/parseAffectsTextLine/parseImproveLine/parseScanLine against the entire corpus, not just pre-filtered matches. Zero real runtime errors found. Two functions (parseLunarLine, parseCreatureLoreLine) initially threw on nearly every call ("attempt to index local 'moon' (a nil value)", "bad argument #1 to 'insert' (table expected, got nil)") -- investigated rather than assumed either way: confirmed both are always preceded by beginLunar()/beginCreatureLore() in the real trigger flow (lunarBegin/loreStart handlers), re-ran with that setup against the full corpus, zero errors -- test-harness gap, not a real bug. This completes the DataLayer portion of the real-code emulation effort; ChatTriggers route() destinations and the Geyser-dependent render/display layer remain for a future pass, and GMCP-dependent logic can't be exercised this way at all since GMCP was never in the text logs.
2026-07-06 fix: traced the actual current-state pipeline before fixing anything (route() -> demonnic.chat:append(tabName) -> EMCO's xEcho('a' mode) copies the current triggering line, color preserved, no gag) then fixed all five confirmed gaps from today's audit. MyDSL_ChatTriggers.lua: full rewrite of every route() pattern, verified against the clean corpus per-pattern via a standalone script (not just eyeballed) -- CONFIRMED real text for tells (both directions)/local says/gossip/yell/whisper, all using "verb(?: (Language))? 'message'" (quote-terminated, optional language tag -- confirmed present on tells/gossips/yells alike) instead of the old invented colon convention; INFERRED (same convention, no direct example yet but DSL_Helpfiles/ confirms the command family) for shout and cgossip; dropped kingdom-says/auctions/NEWBIE/congratulates/QUEST/prays/group-chat/OOC entirely rather than replace one guess with another -- "prays" in particular turned out to be a travel-spell cast message ("Aothien prays for transportation!"), not a chat line at all, so the original pattern would have actively misrouted gameplay text into a chat tab if it had ever matched. MyDSL_DataLayer.lua: fixed parseWeatherLine()'s keyword filter from plain substring matching to a word-boundary Lua pattern (%f[%a]word%f[%A]) -- substring matching meant "sun" matched inside "Sunday", confirmed via corpus search to be actively corrupting MyDSL.State.weather with the log-session-start banner every time a new log file opened; verified the fix via luajit against the full 328,643-line corpus (false positives rejected, real weather kept, would-update count dropped from 33,801 to 687). MyDSL_TargetView.lua: considerLine1/considerLine2 (invented text, matched nothing) replaced with considerEasyKill/considerNoMatch built from the only two confirmed-real consider-output tiers found in the corpus ("X looks like an easy kill."/"X is no match for you.") -- other difficulty tiers DSL likely has are left for future confirmation rather than guessed. Added MyDSL.help() + "mydsl help" alias to MyDSL_DataLayer.lua (static command list) and MyDSL.Route.setHistoryFont() + "mydsl history font <n>" alias to MyDSL_RouteHelper.lua, closing both confirmed alias gaps from the prior audit. All four edited files syntax-checked via luajit loadfile(). None of this confirmed in-game yet -- per project workflow, TODO.md items stay open until Steven confirms live.
- 2026-07-07: TargetView no longer renders/sends murder or order-all-attack against your own group members or followers (checked by name against the live group roster); fixes the "clicked bear from group, got kill option" safety bug. Verified via emulation test, needs live confirm.

Verified against a real captured room block, needs live confirm.

Verified via emulation tests simulating a full restart, needs live confirm.
- 2026-07-07: GroupView member-name truncation now uses AffectsView's cutText() convention (trailing ~ marker) instead of a flat mid-word sub(1,20) cut. Verified against the real 22-char mob name that exposed the bug, needs live confirm.
- 2026-07-07: found and fixed the likely root cause of "order all kill greaser did not work from target window": MyDSL_TargetView.lua's murder/order_attack actions always sent the murder verb (DSL's PKILL-only player-attack command per DSL_Helpfiles), never kill/hit, even against mob targets -- confirmed via the real native (oac) alias script (a trivial order-all-kill passthrough, not the bug source itself) and DSL_Helpfiles/"kill kill command hit.txt". Both actions now pick kill vs murder by t.is_mob; MyDSL_GroupView.lua quickAction() fixed to pass is_mob through too. Verified via emulation test, needs live confirm.
- 2026-07-07: investigated the long-standing "chat capture bug" (grabs first letter of following line, blamed on a GMCP S echo) -- checked every gmcp.* event handler in MyDSL_DataLayer.lua, none touch the raw text stream/selectCurrentLine/deleteLine at all, so no code-level mechanism found. Left open, redirected to MyDSL_RawCapture.lua for a live repro next time it happens rather than guessing further with no reproducible case.
- 2026-07-07: fixed the low-contrast blue action-button complaint -- MyDSL_TargetView.lua's Rescue/Cr.Blind/Cr.Disease/Cr.Poison/Cr.Fatigue/Cr.Bugbite buttons (and GroupView's quick-action Rescue, same shared color) changed from pale blue-violet 170,170,255 to a more legible soft cyan/teal 120,210,220; left the separate mob/player type-indicator blue alone since that's a category color, not an action. Needs live confirm.
- 2026-07-07: character-bound the three remaining shared-file settings gaps -- MyDSL_ChatWrapper.lua chat_settings.lua, MyDSL_LayoutEngine.lua MyDSL_layout.lua, MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua MyDSL_windowstate.lua all now per-character files, matching the recorded all-settings-character-bound decision. Also added MyDSL.Layout.resetAll() + "mydsl layout reset" alias, and wrapped both saveState()/Layout.save() in pcall with a debug log on failure. All three files syntax-checked.
- 2026-07-07: ThemeEngine setOverride() now validates the key against MyDSL.Theme.defaults instead of silently accepting anything; logs and returns false on an unknown key. Verified via emulation test.
- 2026-07-07: chat capture bug (grabbed first letter of following line) confirmed fixed per Steven live testing. No specific code-level cause was ever pinned down; closing on live confirmation rather than a diagnosed root cause.
- 2026-07-07: fixed a real "Unknown" character-binding gap per Steven's question -- every character-bound module (ChatWrapper, LayoutEngine, WindowRegistry, TargetView, CombatView, History font) loaded settings at script-boot time, which happens before login on a fresh Mudlet start, so they'd get stuck on "Unknown"'s bucket/defaults for the whole session even after the real character logged in. MyDSL_DataLayer.lua's gmcp.login_data handler now raises MyDSL.character.identified; all affected modules re-load+apply on it. AffectsView needed no fix, already self-heals via checkCharacterProfile() on every render. Verified end-to-end via emulation test (boot under Unknown -> fire event -> real saved settings load correctly), all 7 files syntax-checked.
- 2026-07-07: cataloging pass for TargetView debuffs -- weaken/slow now confirmed with 4 independent real examples each (different mobs, identical template), ready to build. blindness/poison/plague confirmed as real cast-spells via DSL_Helpfiles but have zero confirmed on-target success text anywhere in the 93-file DSL2-era log corpus (only self-directed "You are blinded!" found); no wears-off text found for any of the five maladictions. Documented in TODO.md, nothing built yet.
- 2026-07-07: added mydsl who <name> as a real passthrough to the native DslColors dslcolor show <name> command (calls dslColorCommand() directly, not send(), since send() would just hit the server as an unknown command rather than re-trigger a native alias). pcall-guarded, verified via isolated logic test. Needs live confirm.
- 2026-07-07: skill/spell action-text cataloging pass for the "Skills/Spells -> Combat window" idea (216-file DSL2-era log corpus, found a previously-missed log/2026-0/ subdirectory). Found the item's two seed examples ("You disarm Jhawsh!", "You kick!") do not actually exist in DSL2-era logs -- likely misremembered. Confirmed real replacements instead: waylay, riot, frenzy, swarm, castigation, dragon roar, an 8-variant killing-blow flavor-text pool, and a bash-specific evasion form -- all added to docs/DSL_CommandRef.md. - 2026-07-08: found round 1 of the RightHere-on-look fix wasn't actually sufficient -- notes_utf8.txt flagged "look command does not update the righthere window with mobs" and "blank lines are still present after the sommoned/followers bear wolf elementals" as still happening post-fix. This -- not the blank line -- is what "the space after certain followers" actually meant. Fixed: broadened parseLookHereLine's name match with an article-anchored generic "here"/"in the room" fallback, reordered the fixture check ahead of it to avoid item lines ("X lies here.") being miscaptured as mobs, and added isCharmedStatusLine() as a safety net so any "(Charmed)"-tagged line is treated as skip-don't-end regardless of verb phrasing, covering future never-seen verbs too. Verified via emulation against the real captured sequences from both rounds -- all entities now captured, capture never terminates early.
- 2026-07-08: found the ACTUAL root cause of RightHere-on-look, round 3 -- Steven reported it was still not updating live even after rounds 1 and 2. Both those fixes were real but moot: the trigger that calls beginLook(), anchored on "^\[Exits: .*\]\s*$", has never matched a single real line, ever -- corpus-checked all 172 distinct "Exits:" lines across the whole log history and every single one has a leading space before the bracket that the anchor's start-of-line "^\[" rejects. My emulation testing in rounds 1/2 called MyDSL.beginLook() directly, which bypassed this trigger entirely and could never have caught it. Fixed the anchor to tolerate leading whitespace; hardened the same bug class in scanAround's "Looking around you see:" anchor too (9 of 412 real occurrences have a leading double-space). Also ran a broader 718-line corpus sweep of the round-2 fallback and confirmed it correctly (and necessarily) catches static room-landmark lines too ("A large beautiful fountain flows here.", "A member of the Minotaur Guard stands watch here.") -- these sit directly in the real presence-listing, same bug class as charmed pets, just far more common. Found and fixed one new false positive from the broadened fallback: "You see a long and sharp Protection Sword here." was being miscaptured as a mob; added "You see ... here" (9 real occurrences) to isLookFixtureLine(). Verified the full anchor-to-body chain end to end via emulation this time, exercising the real installed trigger IDs rather than calling internal functions directly -- closes the exact testing gap that let this hide through two prior "fixed and verified" rounds. Documented one known remaining lower-priority gap: a handful of proper-named NPCs use non-standard verb phrasing with no article to anchor on ("Sorbus the Hermit is sitting here...").
- 2026-07-08: Steven reported RightHere still not updating even after the round-3 anchor fix, with a fresh log showing more never-before-seen charmed-follower phrasing ("A wild wolverine is foaming at the mouth.", "A wild cougar is here growling at you."). Re-checked the exact anchor+body chain against this new log via emulation -- correctly handled, capture never terminates early. Also ruled out: dofile path divergence (native XML's dofile path resolves to the identical file via diff/inode check), and window visibility (Kien's persisted windowstate has MyDSL_RightHere = true). No further bug found from static analysis alone. Added `mydsl righthere dump` to MyDSL_ScanView.lua -- a diagnostic alias that echoes the raw scan.rightHere table straight to the main console, bypassing the window, to distinguish a capture problem from a display problem. Waiting on Steven to run it live and report back.
- 2026-07-08: `mydsl righthere dump` came back 0 entries/capture inactive on every real look. Fixed a bug in the dump alias itself first (used cecho() with decho's RGB-triple color syntax, printed the literal tag instead of rendering). Added a temporary debug trace directly to the trigger chain (anchor fire, beginLook() entry, capture success, endLook() entry) since static analysis had exhausted itself; Steven ran one more real test with it in place. Found the ACTUAL root cause immediately: endLook() was firing on the exact same "[Exits: ...]" line that had just fired lookExits and installed the capture body catch-all, every single time. Root cause: lookBody's ".*" catch-all gets installed by lookExits's callback while that same line is still being processed, and Mudlet evaluates newly-registered triggers against the current line in the same pass -- so the brand-new lookBody immediately re-fires on the very Exits line that created it, which isn't recognized as fixture/mob/charm/continuation, so it falls through to "unrelated event, end capture" and closes immediately, before any real content line is ever seen. This is why rounds 1-3 were all individually correct but the feature never actually worked, ever, in real gameplay. Fixed with one line: lookBody now explicitly treats a line matching the Exits-line shape itself as a keep-capturing no-op. Verified via emulation reproducing the exact self-retrigger scenario -- capture survives, all real entities get captured. Removed the temporary debug traces.

Verified the corrected pattern matches both quiet and non-quiet real prompt text. Also flagged in passing: the live trigger's script is a bare deleteLine(), not gated by MyDSL.Prompt.enabled the way MyDSL_PromptView.lua's own header comment describes -- a doc/reality drift, not fixed, just noted.

- 2026-07-09: Steven asked how to change the roller's goal from 241, then reported "roller is not pausing to let me input." Traced the character-creation log in detail and found MyDSL_Roller.lua (ported 2026-07-07) and a native trigger literally named "roller" were both reacting to every stat-roll line independently, each with a separate goal variable that could drift out of sync -- confirmed via a literal artifact (the native trigger's old-style `</cyan>` closing tag doesn't parse in Mudlet and leaks out as visible text) showing duplicated output on the first ~14 rolls. If the two goals disagree, one trigger can decide "pause" while the other, on a stale goal, decides "reject" and sends n anyway, silently overriding the pause. Turned out Steven had already independently noticed something was off and manually deleted the native "roller" trigger mid-session (confirmed: its fingerprint vanishes in the log at exactly that point) -- the actual "not pausing" moment he'd flagged was him manually using ~ to stop the roller himself to keep a good roll, not a real bug. From the deletion point on, MyDSL_Roller.lua alone handled every roll correctly. Closed the Roller TODO item -- confirmed working, `set goal <n>` is the one command that matters now.

Verified the revert via emulation: Coliseum lines are correctly excluded again, normal combat tracking is unaffected.

- 2026-07-10: found and fixed RightHere-on-look round 7, a real bug hiding in Steven's note "the righthere window is recording my ch[a]r[a]cter on olyndros" -- turned out to describe a screenshot showing RightHere completely empty in "The Hill-lands" despite a charmed mount and 2 hill dwarves clearly present. Root cause: the room's static landmark lines are sometimes indented ("     A twisted and gnarled pine tree grows crookedly here."), and both parseLookHereLine() and isUnparsedPresenceLine() ran their ^-anchored checks against the raw, untrimmed line, so the leading whitespace meant the checks never matched and capture ended on the very first indented line. isLookFixtureLine() never had this problem since its checks are unanchored substrings. Fixed by trimming both functions' input before pattern-matching. Verified via emulation against the exact real sequence -- all 4 entities now captured correctly.
- 2026-07-11: matched damage-flag colors to PNP exactly, per Steven's note ("change the colors of the damage flags to the same as PNP for community recognition"). Read PNP's real dam_info/flag_info tables (DSL_PNP_Battle.lua) and color_table RGB values (DSL_PNP_Support.lua) and replaced every guessed color in DAM_INFO/FLAG_COLOR (MyDSL_DataLayer.lua) with PNP's exact values. Also discovered and added something we were missing entirely: PNP's GHASTLY-through-UNSPEAKABLE severity tier bakes a letter-by-letter alternating red/white color effect directly into the decorated verb text, which our old implementation rendered as one flat color instead. Added a new alternateLetters() helper that builds this at load time and an info.decorated field consumed by both calcDamVerb() (round-summary) and parseCombatDamageLine() (per-swing feed). Verified via the emulation harness: UNSPEAKABLE renders the correct alternating-color letter sequence, ordinary tiers render PNP's exact green/yellow/red. Needs a live fight to visually confirm.
- 2026-07-11: found and fixed a real GroupView bug from Steven's note "follower not showing in group for vaelis (untrained guardhand)". Checked the actual log (log/2026-07-11#07-41-33.html) and found the real group block: "Vaelis's group:" / "[ 1 Mob] An untrained guardhand ..." / "[ 1 Mag] Vaelis ...". DSL right-justifies the level in a fixed-width field, so level-1 members get a leading space before the number that higher levels (e.g. "[51 War] Olyndros ...") don't -- parseGroupLine()'s pattern required a digit immediately after "[", so it silently failed on any line with that leading space, which meant the WHOLE group vanished (Vaelis herself included, also level 1), not just the follower -- Steven just happened to notice the follower's absence first. Fixed by adding %s* after the opening bracket. Verified via emulation against the exact real 3-line block (guardhand/Vaelis/a no-space control line) -- all 3 now parse correctly.
- 2026-07-11: consolidated timers per Steven's "can we pull all timers off one tick" question, and fixed "improve timer not counting down." Audited every tempTimer() in the profile -- only 2 self-perpetuating loops existed: TickSource's own 0.25s T.loop() (left alone, TickView needs it for smooth bar animation) and MoonWeather's separate tempTimer(1, loop) clock chain. Added a new MyDSL.Timers.Slow event (throttled to 1/sec inside T.updateTimer()) and moved AffectsView's redraw, LiveView's render(), and MoonWeather's clock onto it -- all three were either redrawing 4x/sec for a countdown that only ever shows whole seconds, or (MoonWeather) running an entirely separate timer to get the same once/sec cadence this already produces internally. Deleted MoonWeather's startClockTimer()/stopClockTimer() entirely. Built the actual Improve fix: new improveLiveText() in MyDSL_LiveView.lua recomputes "M online minutes to improvement" live from elapsed real seconds since last_updated (confirmed via DSL_Helpfiles/improve improvement.txt that this is wall-clock online time, not a DSL tick interval, so deliberately not using TickSource's tick-average conversion) -- now actually counts down instead of freezing at the last-read value. Found and fixed a real bonus bug in the same plumbing: MyDSL_DataBridge.lua's sync() was rebuilding MyDSL.DB.tick from MyDSL.State.tick (which never holds remaining/average/percent, only a time string) on every sync() call -- including ones fired by the same gmcp.tick event TickSource itself reacts to, clobbering TickSource's just-computed smoothed average back to a bare 40 default on every real tick. Fixed by having DataBridge alias the already-correct MyDSL.DB.tick instead of rebuilding it -- TickSource is the sole authority for that table. Verified via emulation: the 1/sec throttle (32 fast pulses -> 8 slow pulses over a simulated 8s span) and the Improve countdown math (mid-countdown + past-due-floors-at-zero cases) both confirmed correct.
- 2026-07-11: found and fixed the real root cause of the recurring EMCO chat "S"/duplicate-line bug, after Steven asked to look into it again and whether chat should be captured via script or "the append demonnic with deleteline." MyDSL_ChatTriggers.lua's route() patterns were completely unanchored (no leading ^) with a quote-crossing ".+" prefix, despite the file's own comments already documenting the real native pattern as anchored -- meaning a verb word appearing INSIDE a different message's quoted dialogue could independently match a second unrelated route() pattern (e.g. "Vaelis tells you 'watch what she says about it'" matched both Tells and Local). Confirmed via Python re against the full 328,643-line clean corpus: 15 real lines matched more than one pattern under the old code, each causing a double append()+deleteLine() fire -- the confirmed mechanism for both a stray-character artifact and an unrelated line getting eaten from the main console. Fixed by anchoring every pattern to line-start and switching every prefix from ".+" to "[^']+"/"[^']*" (can't cross a quote). Merged 3 same-tab pairs that were separately double-matching identical text (gossips/clan gossips, Kingdom:/OOC Kingdom:, dropped a redundant "ask" from the answers/newbie catch-all). Caught one regression before shipping via the same corpus check: the first anchored Kingdom pattern required no speaker name, but all 23 real corpus examples have one ("Sofie Kingdom: '...'") -- fixed to make the name prefix optional. Final result: 0 multi-matches (down from 15), 0 lost coverage. Also incidentally found a separate, unrelated cosmetic bug while checking logs -- literal "&lt;br&gt;" text visible in EMCO's own HTML chat logs, traced to a real bug in vendored EMCOChat/demontools.lua's decho2html() (inserts a literal "<br>" string that then gets HTML-escaped along with everything else) -- left alone since it's vendored, unmodified library code and log-file-only, not visible in the live UI. Answered Steven's architecture question: append+deleteLine already runs through real Mudlet triggers (tempRegexTrigger is the same mechanism the Trigger Editor GUI uses), so "script vs. trigger" wasn't the actual bug axis -- "one line matching N independent triggers at once" was. Did not move to EMCO's native gag-list (checked EMCO:matchesGag() -- Lua patterns only, no alternation, and suppress-only, can't route to a different tab). Also added the explicitly requested gag=false option to route(), applied to say/tells/shout/yell per Steven's ask, so those still route to their tab but also stay visible on the main console.
- 2026-07-11: verified 2 more TOP PRIORITY Combat items while Steven plays to gather logs. (1) Weapon-flag proc attribution via last_attacker/last_target/last_noun: traced parseCombatProcLine() against PNP's handle_flag() directly and found we actually fixed a real bug in PNP's own source along the way -- PNP's drowning/freeze guard compares its `flag` parameter to the literal string "freeze" AFTER already reassigning it to the single-letter code, so that comparison can never be true in PNP itself; our version compares the letter code directly and works correctly. Verified end-to-end via emulation: a damage line sets last_attacker/last_target/last_noun, a following proc line correctly attaches its flag to both the persistent active-fight entry and the round-summary entry. Also reproduced a known, inherent (not new) limitation: in a busy multi-combatant fight, an unrelated swing landing between the real proc cause and its echo will misattribute the flag to whoever swung most recently -- this is PNP's own exact technique (one shared last-event slot, not per-combatant), so it's faithful behavior, not a regression; flagged for Steven's call rather than building a per-noun-tracking improvement unprompted. (2) PNP-faithful display rewrite: confirmed already fully built (mydsl combat mode raw|condensed|gag exists, maps directly onto PNP's own gag_combat/summarize_damage flags) -- this TODO item was stale, just never checked off. Both items closed.
- 2026-07-11: built fully configurable action buttons for TargetView and GroupView, per Steven's answer to 3 open decision questions ("all the buttons should be user configurable... even non standard commands like scan, look, get item etc... manually be able to set all buttons, even the group buttons... there should be a clear button and the mob/player toggle"). Found most of the mechanism already existed (mydsl target mobset/playerset with 6 named slots, a live mob/player [M]/[P] toggle link, mydsl group quickset with 2 slots) -- the real gaps were arbitrary custom commands (buttons could only reference a name from the fixed TV.actions Lua table), no reset-to-default, and a real bug: GroupView's quickset had zero persistence, silently resetting to {"heal","rescue"} on every reload. Added MyDSL.TargetView.defineAction() + "mydsl target action <key> \"<label>\" <color> <command>" (supports %t for the target's name, or no placeholder for stateless commands like scan) -- custom actions are stored in TV.config.custom_actions and merged into the shared TV.actions table every reload, so GroupView's quickset picks them up automatically since it already reads from the same table, no duplicate mechanism needed. Added "mydsl target mobset/playerset reset" and "mydsl group quickset reset", restoring true hardcoded defaults from a freshly-literal TV.defaults/GV.defaults table (deliberately not derived from the live config, so reset can't just restore an already-customized value). Gave GroupView the same configFile()/loadConfig()/saveConfig() per-character persistence pattern TargetView already had, fixing the real quickset-doesn't-persist bug found along the way. Verified via emulation: stateless and %t-substituted custom actions produce the right command, reset restores true defaults without mutating the defaults table, GroupView's quickActions round-trip correctly through reset. This also closed out the original GroupView-heal-tier and TargetView-murder-alt-command TODO items as moot -- Steven can now just point any button at whatever command a character actually knows, no auto-detection machinery needed. Also resolved (no code needed): AffectsView's stealth-tracking mismatch -- confirmed it's per-character saved data, not a code default, and the matching "mydsl affects untrack stealth" alias already exists.

- 2026-07-11: finished the command-surface retrofit's last item -- trimmed the mydsl prefix off Scan/Target/Group/CreatureReference, per Steven ("muscle memory is not a concern... needs to be consistent across all commands, and human speak/readable"). Hard rename, old mydsl-prefixed forms removed entirely. Caught a real collision before shipping: bare "target" would have silently broken a genuine DSL swashbuckler combat skill ("target head/body/legs", confirmed via DSL_Helpfiles/target.txt) -- a bare "^target (.+)$" catch-all would have swallowed it before it ever reached the server. Checked scan/group/lore against their own real helpfiles too -- both safe (no real collision), only target needed a different word. Steven picked "focus" for the whole TargetView module, applied consistently to every sub-command, not just the one that had to change: focus <name>/clear/mobset .../playerset .../mobset reset/playerset reset/action .... GroupView -> group gag/ungag/quickset .../quickset reset. ScanView -> scan gag/ungag (mydsl righthere dump, a debug diagnostic, deliberately left alone). CreatureReference -> lore <name|hide|show>. Updated mydsl help's static list to match. Found and fixed a second real bug while renaming: the bare focus (.+)$ catch-all also matched every one of TargetView's own specific sub-commands since ".+" matches their arguments too -- Mudlet fires every alias whose pattern matches, not just the most specific one, so a real "focus mobset murder consider ..." command was ALSO silently setting the target to a mob literally named "mobset murder consider ..." every time. Existed under the old "mydsl target (.+)$" name too, just never surfaced -- the old guard only excluded the literal word "clear," missing the other three reserved words. Fixed by checking the first word against the full reserved list. Verified via emulation: all 4 modules load cleanly with the renamed aliases registered, and the reserved-word guard correctly excludes every sub-command while still setting real targets normally.
