- 2026-07-11: ran /code-review (8-angle, high effort) against the whole session's work (12 commits, base ce7ea20), then fixed everything it found. Critical: table.unpack() doesn't exist in Mudlet's real embedded Lua runtime (only bare unpack) -- confirmed by mounting the actual Mudlet AppImage and grepping its bundled mudlet-lua library directly (zero table.unpack anywhere, unpack(...) used throughout, matching every vendored PNP file's own convention) -- every emulation test this session passed using a standalone luajit binary that apparently provides table.unpack as an extra global Mudlet's sandbox doesn't have, so focus/group reset commands would have crashed live despite passing every test. Fixed both occurrences (MyDSL_TargetView.lua, MyDSL_GroupView.lua). Real reachable bug: toggle moons/toggle ticktimer could get stuck doing the opposite of what's needed after a reload, since MW.toggle()/V.toggle() decided from a local config.shown flag instead of WindowRegistry's real state -- confirmed loadState() bypasses both entirely, and V.config.shown has its own separate save file. Fixed by delegating to MyDSL.Windows.show/hide/toggle() (MoonWeather) and preferring the real registry state when available (TickView), verified via emulation against the exact desync scenario. Confirmed structural fragility: TargetView's focus catch-all guard was a hardcoded reserved-word list independent of the actual registered sub-commands -- the same desync had already happened once. Fixed by co-locating each sub-command's reserved word with its own alias registration instead of a separate list. Cleanup: combatMode's 3 near-identical if-blocks collapsed into one lookup table; new MyDSL.charName()/MyDSL.safeFileName()/MyDSL.copyArray() (MyDSL_DataLayer.lua) replace what had become 3-4 independent copies of the same logic across CombatView/TargetView/GroupView; docs/MyDSL_MudletAPIReference.md's dated bug-fix table got a note clarifying its command examples are pre-rename. All fixes verified via syntax check + emulation before commit.
- 2026-07-11: built a "normalize the look" visual-design pass for Steven, then wired it in for real. First presented 3 concrete visual directions (Refined Convergence, Terminal Purist, Zoned HUD) as an HTML Artifact with real Combat/Affects/Vitals/Target mockups, grounded in a full 13-window styling survey (found 6+ windows with zero background/border at all, Live/Tick duplicating the exact same hardcoded panel style as two independent copies, Location with its own private ad-hoc theme table, and MyDSL_ThemeEngine.lua sitting fully built but completely unconsumed -- zero callers of MyDSL.Theme.get() anywhere). After Steven reviewed 10 ChatGPT concept renders he'd generated separately and asked "can we make a few different themes off what you provided? and some from the gpt images?", built it for real: added 5 named presets to MyDSL_ThemeEngine.lua (the 3 Artifact options, plus obsidian_ember/arcane_midnight adapted from 2 of the ChatGPT renders that needed zero new art assets) with MyDSL.Theme.setTheme()/.list(), a shared (not character-bound) persistence file, and a theme/theme list/theme set <name> alias. Discovered MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua's applyTheme() placeholder was flatly wrong -- its comment claimed Geyser.UserWindow doesn't support setStyleSheet, but Mudlet's real source (GeyserUserWindow.lua:91) confirms it does, wrapping the native setUserWindowStyleSheet() and documented as theming exactly "border and title area." Made applyTheme() real for all 14 UserWindow registry entries at once, which is what actually makes a single setTheme() call reach every window. Added MyDSL.Theme.styleConsole() and used it to theme the 8 windows that previously had no background/border/font at all (Affects, Combat, Target, Group, Scan, RightHere, PlayersNear, CreatureReference), then migrated the windows with existing bespoke styling (Live, Tick, Portrait, Location, MoonWeather) to pull chrome colors from the active theme, deliberately leaving semantic colors (HP/mana/move gradients, tick urgency palette, damage-tier colors) untouched since those carry meaning independent of visual theme. Chat's outer window chrome themes too via the same universal registry fix; its inner EMCO tab styling was left alone this pass (separate, more complex system). Verified with a dedicated emulation harness covering all 5 presets, zone differentiation, override precedence, and settings persistence across a simulated reload.
- 2026-07-11: confirmed the new ThemeEngine live in-game, after chasing down a false alarm first. Steven's first two screenshots (theme set refined_convergence, then theme set terminal_purist) showed zero visible change anywhere, including on Live/Tick which were definitely migrated to read from the theme -- looked like a real bug. Root cause traced to a stale mid-session reload, not the new code: every module in this codebase guards its event-handler setup with an "already installed" flag living on the persistent MyDSL.* globals, which survive a script-file reload by design (so Geyser windows don't get recreated on every save). Since those modules' installHandlers() had already run earlier in the same session, before today's edits existed on disk, reloading the .lua files didn't re-run them -- the brand-new "MyDSL.theme.changed" listeners never actually registered until a genuine process restart. Worth remembering for any future change that adds a new registerAnonymousEventHandler call to an already-initialized module. After Steven restarted Mudlet and retried, both themes "worked as intended" -- confirmed visually distinct, correct borders/colors/fonts. Closes the live-confirmation gap for all 5 presets (the other 3 share the identical code path with different color values).

- 2026-07-11: four fixes to the new LiveView v1A15 layout from Steven's first live screenshot. Reverted the bar fill's corner rounding back to fully-rounded (matching the track underneath) -- the brief left-only rounding looked mismatched at 100% fill, which read as "changed from the previous style." Made the exits MiniConsole resize to roughly match the exits text length instead of sitting in a fixed-width box, since MiniConsole has no setStyleSheet to strip its native Qt frame directly. Found and fixed a real, confirmed bug along the way: MyDSL_DataBridge.lua's sync() unconditionally rebuilt the whole MyDSL.DB.room table on every call, silently wiping the exitsColoredSource/exitsLine fields LiveView's colored-exits capture sets -- since sync() runs far more often than a fresh room description scrolls by, the flag cleared within moments of being set, so the next render tick always fell back to the plain-white version. This is why the exits line "changed from original game text to white text" -- the colored capture was working, just getting immediately clobbered. Fixed by preserving those fields across the rebuild, same pattern already used for DB.tick.

- 2026-07-11: CRITICAL project-wide fix -- table.load() was being called wrong in almost every persistence function in the codebase. Steven's direct question ("are the settings loading at creating from save files or they saving and never reading/updating?") led to checking Mudlet's own bundled source directly: table.load(file, target) returns NOTHING -- it unpickles saved data into an explicit second-argument table (or _G if none given), confirmed independently via PNP's and EMCO's own real source both always passing a destination table. Almost every "local data = table.load(path)" call in this codebase (no second argument) was silently getting nil back, every time, forever -- meaning theme persistence, window visibility, the character-data restore (MyDSL.load()), and several other config loads never actually survived a real restart despite being "confirmed working" (which only ever verified in-session behavior, never a genuine restart). Fixed in MyDSL_DataLayer.lua, MyDSL_ThemeEngine.lua, MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua (both visibility state and the new font store), MyDSL_CreatureLore.lua, MyDSL_TargetView.lua, MyDSL_GroupView.lua, MyDSL_CombatView.lua, MyDSL_LayoutEngine.lua, MyDSL_PromptView.lua. Also found why this session's own tests never caught it: every prior test used a hand-rolled table.save/table.load stub that (unlike the real thing) incorrectly returned the loaded data directly. Built a faithful replica of Mudlet's actual algorithm (copied from its bundled source, confirmed to reproduce the exact real on-disk file format) and re-ran every persistence test against it -- several failed against the old code and pass against the fix, proving both the bug and the repair. MyDSL_TickView.lua/MyDSL_LiveView.lua/MyDSL_AlterformView.lua checked and confirmed unaffected (they use their own io.open+dofile persistence instead).
- 2026-07-11: removed Combat window's scrollbar (per Steven) -- turned out to be the original, never-touched value from Combat's first version, missed by the earlier pass that removed scrollbars from History/PlayersNear/Scan/RightHere/Target/Group for consistency. Also experimentally extended MyDSL.Theme.panelCSS() with a QDockWidget::title CSS rule, reusing each preset's already-defined (previously unwired) titleColor/titleBgColor/titleFont/titleFontSize fields, to test whether Mudlet's native UserWindow title bars can be colored and shrunk (tight 2px vertical padding) via CSS -- applied automatically through the existing applyTheme() path, needs a live screenshot to confirm since Mudlet's C++ implementation of setUserWindowStyleSheet() isn't visible from the Lua side to verify in advance.
- 2026-07-11: title-bar CSS round 2. Confirmed via pixel-sampling a live screenshot that round 1's approach (appending QDockWidget::title directly onto panelCSS()'s bare declarations in one string) did not work -- every window's title bar background was Mudlet's exact default gray (53,53,53) and text was plain white, while the window border color was correctly themed, proving the mixing of bare declarations with a trailing selector block in one Qt stylesheet string is invalid. Split into two functions: panelCSS() reverted to its original bare-declarations-only form (used by Label panels), new windowChromeCSS() is all selector-qualified rules (QDockWidget { ... } QDockWidget::title { ... }), wired into applyTheme() for real native UserWindow objects only. Added a test assertion confirming windowChromeCSS()'s output has no bare declarations before its first selector brace, so this class of mistake can't silently regress again. Still unverified whether Mudlet's UserWindow chrome responds to QDockWidget selectors at all -- needs another live screenshot.
- 2026-07-12: researched round 2's title-bar CSS against Mudlet's real C++ source before another live test, per Steven's explicit ask. Confirmed via src/TDockWidget.h (Mudlet/Mudlet GitHub) that TDockWidget is a thin QDockWidget subclass with no custom title-bar widget and no paintEvent override, so QDockWidget/QDockWidget::title selectors are structurally correct; independently confirmed via GitHub PR #4046 (the PR that added setUserWindowStyle()/Geyser :setStyleSheet() support for UserWindows), whose own official example uses the exact same two-full-selector-block shape windowChromeCSS() already produces. That PR's discussion also flagged a real Qt/Mudlet limitation: border styling on a UserWindow only persists while floating, not while docked -- title-bar styling alone works in both states. Steven then tested live and confirmed exactly that split (floating: works: docked: doesn't).

Deleted MyDSL.Theme.windowChromeCSS() (MyDSL_ThemeEngine.lua) and reverted WindowRegistry's applyTheme() back to its pre-experiment form (plain panelCSS() call, no windowChromeCSS()/fallback branching). titleCSS() (predates this experiment, still unused elsewhere) and the preset titleColor/titleBgColor/titleFont/titleFontSize fields (predate this experiment too) left untouched. - 2026-07-12: fixed a real bug found live in AlterformView (Steven: "everything seems to work, just the window needs to not be visible unless the alterform affect is active. you can see the min/close buttons"). Read Mudlet's actual bundled GeyserAdjustableContainer.lua (via the mounted AppImage) and confirmed the constructor's lockStyle="padding" field has done nothing since it was written: Mudlet only calls lockContainer() internally if locked=true is ALSO passed at creation (never was), and "padding" isn't even a real lockStyle name ("standard"/"border"/"full"/"light" are the only valid ones) -- it silently fell back to fully unlocked, meaning the min/restore/close/lock/save/load buttons were always live and visible regardless of the countdown's own active-based show/hide state. Fixed in MyDSL_AlterformView.lua by calling the real API, lockContainer("light"), after creation -- Mudlet's own documented style for hiding just the min/restore and close labels without affecting borders/margin. MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua has the identical dead lockStyle="padding" pattern and likely the same bug -- flagged in TODO.md, not fixed yet pending Steven's confirmation he wants it there too.
- 2026-07-12: applied the same lockContainer("light") fix to MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua, per Steven ("yes fix moonweather too") -- identical dead lockStyle="padding" pattern, same root cause as AlterformView's fix above. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed a second, distinct real bug found live immediately after the lockContainer("light") fix (Steven: "the min/close buttons were off till kien loaded in then they appeared again"). Not a theme or save-file issue. Root cause: MyDSL_AlterformView.lua/MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua both build their own Adjustable.Container directly but never registered the object back into MyDSL.Windows.registry[name].obj. WindowRegistry's show()/hide() (fired for every registered window on the "MyDSL.character.identified" event) falls back to MyDSL.Windows.ensure() whenever entry.obj is nil -- which, finding no existing object, created a second, fresh, unlocked Adjustable.Container under the same name every time a character identified, silently replacing the first instance's lock state. Fixed in both files by registering entry.obj/entry.created right after building the container, so the fallback reuses the real, already-locked instance instead of recreating a duplicate. Not yet live-confirmed.

- 2026-07-12: built LiveView's in-game age display, per Steven ("looks at score creation date and uses in-game time to tell you when your ingame birthday is and ingame age"). Confirmed via AskUserQuestion he wanted the real in-game-calendar-math version (not just real-world elapsed time), explicitly accepting "approximate" since day/month names aren't continuous across resets. DSL's real-time-to-game-time ratio isn't documented anywhere -- empirically derived it from the full log/ archive: exactly 10 distinct month names ever appear across the whole corpus (10 months/year), day numbers run 1-35 before rolling to the next month (35 days/month), and ~35 real minutes = 1 in-game day (trimmed mean of 64 clean single-day-step samples, timestamped against real log file mtimes, discarding AFK/offline-gap outliers). Parses score's "Created:" line (both the current full-date format and an older bare-numeric form) in MyDSL_DataLayer.lua, bridges the timestamp through MyDSL_DataBridge.lua, computes age fresh on every render in MyDSL_LiveView.lua's new ageText() (same store-the-anchor-compute-live pattern as improveLiveText()). Placed in the identity row per Steven's own follow-up direction, which also flagged a real space bug: the Religion: (god) field was capturing the full trailing title ("Cliath -=- the God of Creation -=-") instead of just the name, overflowing the row -- fixed to capture just the name (every real god name confirmed single-word via corpus grep + DSL_Helpfiles cross-check), freeing the row for age. - 2026-07-12: researched and built dock/undock with remembered floating geometry, per Steven ("dual docked undocked position memory, research first from web and mudlet manual"). Confirmed Mudlet's native UserWindow layout remembers exactly one geometry per window (not separate docked/undocked profiles), and confirmed there's no reliable Lua event for detecting a native drag-to-dock transition (checked Mudlet's bundled GeyserUserWindow.lua/TDockWidget.h, corroborated by a Mudlet forum thread on the same gap) -- reported this back before building anything. Steven confirmed command/button-driven is fine (matches how he'd already described it -- "push the redock button"), and that size-only (no drag-position) memory is an acceptable partial win. Built MyDSL.Windows.undock()/dock() (MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua Section 8c, new profile-level MyDSL_windowfloat.lua) -- undock() floats the window and restores remembered size/position (or a bigger first-time default); dock() captures the window's real current size via getUserWindowSize() (works even after a native drag-resize) before re-docking, so whatever size Steven left it at persists to next time. No getUserWindowPosition() equivalent exists in Mudlet's API, so position is only remembered when set via undock() itself, not native drag-moves. Deliberately no single toggle command -- no reliable way to query current dock state, so a toggle could only guess and would get it wrong on the second press. Wired as "mydsl location undock/dock" and "mydsl portrait undock/dock". Not yet live-confirmed.

- 2026-07-12: found and fixed two real bugs in the dock/undock feature from Steven's live testing (screenshots showed the actual behavior). (1) Redock was landing in the lower-right corner instead of back in place, requiring a manual drag to fix -- root cause: dock() called winObj:setDockPosition("right"), and Geyser's own bundled source confirms setDockPosition() explicitly passes restoreLayout=false to Mudlet's native openUserWindow(), forcing a generic right-edge dock instead of restoring the actual saved layout position -- it landed wherever Mudlet's own stacking order put a fresh right-dock request, not back where it was. Fixed by calling openUserWindow(name, true, autoDock) directly instead (the same pattern Geyser.UserWindow:enableAutoDock() itself uses internally) -- restoreLayout=true snaps it back to the real saved position, no dockPosition guessing needed. (2) Undock inconsistently skipped applying the saved size ("the alias works for resizing and placement of undock, but t[he] undock just undocs and doesnt change position or size") -- best theory is a race between setDockPosition("floating") starting the docked->floating transition and :move()/:resize() (which Geyser's own docstrings say only work once a window is actually floating) firing before that transition finished. Added a 0.2s tempTimer delay before applying the saved geometry. Flagged this second fix as the less certain of the two -- if it still misses sometimes, that would point away from a timing issue and toward something else. Both confirmed VIT/Chamber working correctly in the same live session (visible in the screenshot). Not yet re-confirmed after these two fixes.
- 2026-07-12: reverted the whole dock/undock-with-remembered-geometry feature, per Steven ("revert back docking changes, its not working as intended and seems to be not worth the effort, manually adjusting is fine... the docking code we just created not the whole UI docking"). Removed MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua's Section 8c (undock()/dock()/floatGeometry store), the "mydsl location undock/dock" and "mydsl portrait undock/dock" aliases and their help-text lines, and deleted the now-unused MyDSL_windowfloat.lua. Mudlet's native window docking/dragging is untouched -- only the custom command-driven save/restore layer built earlier today is gone. The unrelated fixes from the same session (dragon Vitality/Chamber confirmed working, Age display font size) are unaffected by this revert.
- 2026-07-12: built MoonWeather's weather display, per Steven ("across all the logs do we have enough weather info to add it to the top of the moonweather window above the moon images... need suggestions"). The data side already existed -- MyDSL_DataLayer.lua's parseWeatherLine() has captured MyDSL.State.weather.description from the real `weather` command for a while, and MoonWeather already listened for MyDSL.weather.updated to re-render, it just never actually read the text anywhere. Confirmed real taxonomy via corpus grep across 101 captured `weather` outputs: Clear/Scattered clouds/Lightning-Storm/Rain/Sleet/Snow, each with distinct day and night wording (day/night picked from whether "night" appears in the actual captured sentence, no dependency on this module's own separate day/night tracking). Presented 3 concrete display options via AskUserQuestion (symbol+text, text-only, symbol-only) since Steven explicitly asked for suggestions rather than guessing at a visual I can't preview myself -- he picked symbol + short label. Added buildWeatherText() (MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua) and a new row above the moon circles; rebalanced the table's row-height proportions (was 50/20/30 for moons/focal-text/time, now 12/44/18/26 with the new weather row) to fit it. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed the weather icons not rendering, found live same day (Steven: "no icons heres text to compare Rain falls steadily from the clouded sky. and a cold gentle breeze blows in from the north."). Confirmed the description text itself was captured correctly (matches the corpus taxonomy, "rain" keyword matched) -- the icons themselves just weren't showing. Root cause: 🌧/🌨 (U+1F327/U+1F328) are in Unicode's newer "Supplemental Symbols and Pictographs" block (introduced 2014), which doesn't have guaranteed font coverage on Linux without a full emoji font installed. Switched the whole icon set to the much older, near-universally-supported "Miscellaneous Symbols"/"Dingbats" blocks (Unicode 1.1/3.0, 1993-1999) -- same family as the already-working ⚡/❄/☀/⛅/☁. Sleet now reuses the snow symbol (no equivalently old sleet-specific character exists; the text label disambiguates, matching why Steven picked symbol+text over symbol-only originally); clear-night switched from ✨ (also newer, Unicode 6.0) to ☆ (Unicode 1.1) for the same reason. Also noted, not fixed: the live wind detail arrives on a separate line starting lowercase "and" ("and a cold gentle breeze blows in from the north"), which the weather trigger (^[A-Z][^.]+\.$) doesn't match -- low priority since wind isn't part of the displayed row at all currently.
- 2026-07-12: added wind to the MoonWeather display, per Steven ("wind should be captured. clouds, clear, rain, gold [cold] breeze, temperate wind, etc"). Confirmed a complete taxonomy via corpus grep -- 96 real samples across the full log/ archive (193 files): temperature {cold, temperate, warm}, strength {gentle, moderate}, direction {north, south, east, west}, plus a calm/no-wind form -- no other values found anywhere, confirming this isn't a partial sample. Added MyDSL.extractWindClause() (MyDSL_DataLayer.lua) which pulls the wind portion out of the same sentence parseWeatherLine() already captures -- covers the standard comma-joined form, confirmed 53/53 real samples take this shape. Added a narrow second trigger (weatherWindContinuation) for the rare period-joined continuation case Steven's own live example hit (0 historical occurrences of that exact shape anywhere in the archive, but confirmed real) -- routes directly to windDescription only, so it can't overwrite the precipitation description parseWeatherLine() already captured moments before. MoonWeather's new windLabel() (MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua) renders it as a compact 2-word label (noun picked from strength -- "gentle" always paired with "breeze" and "moderate" with "wind" in all 53 real samples checked, e.g. "Cold Breeze"/"Warm Wind"/"Calm") appended after the precipitation icon+label with a bullet separator. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: wind display confirmed live -- screenshot showed "Cloudy • Cold Breeze" with the wind label exactly right, but caught a second icon-font miss: ⛅ (Cloudy/day) showed no glyph, same failure mode as the earlier 🌧. This time traced the actual Unicode version instead of re-guessing: ⛅ (U+26C5) is Unicode 5.2 (2009), wrongly grouped earlier with the genuinely-old (1993) ☀/☁/❄/☂/☆ -- verified all 5 of those individually via compart.com, confirmed real Unicode 1.1. Fixed by using plain ☁ for both day and night Cloudy, since no old-block day-specific sun+cloud glyph exists. Also checked ⚡ (Storm): Unicode 4.0 (2003) -- older than the emoji block that failed, probably fine, but genuinely not yet live-tested since no storm weather has come up during testing -- flagged rather than swapped preemptively.
- 2026-07-12: root-caused and fixed the whole font problem, per Steven ("is there a font better for mudlet and visual items. currently using bitstream vera sans mono. research this?" then "fix all font issues. can we install better fonts?"). Checked directly via fc-list: none of the fonts referenced anywhere -- Mudlet's main-window font (Bitstream Vera Sans Mono) nor MyDSL's own CSS fallback chains ("DejaVu Sans Mono", "Ubuntu Mono") -- were actually installed on this system at all; every reference had been silently falling through to a generic default this whole time, which is the real underlying cause behind both icon-rendering misses this session. Noto Sans Mono (many weights) plus Noto Sans Symbols/Noto Sans Symbols 2/Noto Color Emoji/Noto Emoji are genuinely installed and built for comprehensive Unicode coverage -- recommended switching to Noto Sans Mono over installing anything new, since its coverage is about as complete as fonts get. Steven switched Mudlet's main font to Noto Sans Mono and confirmed live (screenshot showed MoonWeather's "☆ Clear • Cold Breeze" rendering correctly, the exact glyph class that was failing before). Replaced every "DejaVu Sans Mono"/"Ubuntu Mono" reference across MyDSL_AlterformView.lua, MyDSL_LiveView.lua, MyDSL_TickView.lua, MyDSL_PortraitView.lua, MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua, MyDSL_ThemeEngine.lua with "Noto Sans Mono" (terminal_purist theme's intentional Courier New left untouched). Also clarified: the "Only use symbols (glyphs) from chosen font" checkbox Steven found already unchecked is the Mapper's own 2D-room-symbol-font setting specifically, not general text rendering -- a different feature than the one researched; the font switch itself is what actually fixed it.
- 2026-07-12: bumped LiveView's vitals-bar font size, per Steven ("would like the vitals bar text on either side to be larger (font 9?). HP/Mana/Move and hp/hpmax, mana/manamax, move,movemax. im only changing font size nothing else in there"). barFont already had a live-adjustable control (`mydsl live barfont <n>`, clamped 6-14, wired earlier the same day) -- that's the value "font 9" refers to, so changed the default in MyDSL_LiveView.lua from 8 to 9, and also patched the already-persisted value in MyDSL/live_settings.lua (which would otherwise have kept overriding the new default with the old 8 on next load) to match. No other styling touched, per Steven's explicit scope limit. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed four real bugs in MoonWeather's bonus-line display, per Steven ("it seems to 'redraw' the bonuses sometimes, the timer is not correct and the spacing needs fixing a little. also the fonts need to go up one"). (1) The lunar countdown's hours-remaining math was genuinely wrong -- extracted all 30 distinct real "Cycles remaining X (Y Hours)" pairs from the full log/ corpus (e.g. 1→0, 2→1.5, 45→22, 46→23.5, 65→32) and found the old formula (hours=floor(cyc/2), half whenever cyc is odd) contradicts real DSL text for roughly half of all values -- odd/even alone doesn't determine the half (cycles=45 has no half, cycles=46 does, backwards from what odd/even predicts). The real pattern repeats every 4 cycles: cyc mod 4 == 0 or 1 gives a clean whole-hour count, mod 4 == 2 or 3 adds an extra half -- verified against all 30 real samples with zero exceptions (double-checked with a standalone Lua harness), and cross-checked against MyDSL_state.lua's own captured cycles_remaining=39/hours_remaining=19 pair, which matches. Rewrote MW.countdownStr() with the corrected block-of-4 formula. (2) Spacing bug: buildFocalText()'s Regen/countdown line was missing its <br>, running directly onto the Cs span with no separator ("+2CsRegen+0%") -- added the missing <br>. (3) "Redraw" flicker: MyDSL.Timers.Slow calls MW.render() every real second, but nothing shown needs 1-second resolution (clock only displays minutes, countdown only changes ~every 40s) -- re-echoing identical HTML every second forced Qt to reload the moon <img> tiles from disk every second for no visible benefit. MW.render() now memoizes the last echoed HTML string and skips label:echo() entirely when nothing changed. (4) Fonts bumped +1pt per explicit request: weather/time rows 8pt→9pt, focal/bonus row 7pt→8pt, weather icon 9pt→10pt, base config.fontSize default 9→10 (MoonWeather has no persisted settings file, unlike LiveView, so the default alone is sufficient). Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed the same class of redundant-redraw bug in AffectsView, found from a raw Mudlet debug-window dump Steven shared while asking "does affects need to update that often also?" (same question as the MoonWeather "redraw" bug above). The dump showed the same 8 affect lines being fully re-selectSection()'d/link-rescanned repeatedly with unchanged values. Root cause: MyDSL_AffectsView.lua's onTimersUpdated() unconditionally called A.display() -- its own registerHandlers() comment already documents this as "a full window clear+redraw plus an up-to-300-line link-rescan" -- on every MyDSL.Timers.Slow tick (1/real-second), regardless of display mode. Default timerMode is "cycles", which only shows the bare game-tick count ("Nc") -- that only changes once per real game tick (~40s), and onTickUpdated() already redraws exactly when it does change. So ~39 out of every 40 forced per-second redraws in the default mode were pure wasted repaint of identical text; only "time"/"both" modes show a real-time-interpolated seconds countdown that actually needs 1/sec redraws. onTimersUpdated() now skips A.display() entirely unless timerMode is "time" or "both". Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed a real bug in the earlier "hide min/close buttons" fix for MoonWeather and Alterform, per Steven ("since we locked the window i cant resize the moonweather to fit all the text"). Both modules' button-hiding fix (same day) called container:lockContainer("light"), documented at the time as only hiding those buttons with "borders/margin not affected." Downloaded Mudlet's actual GeyserAdjustableContainer.lua from GitHub (src/mudlet-lua/lua/geyser/) to check: its own doc comment on lockContainer() says point-blank "lock means that your container is no longer moveable/resizable by mouse," and the mouse-event handlers gate purely on self.locked (set true by every lockStyle, "light" included), never on lockStyle itself -- so "light" was silently disabling resize/move the whole time in both MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua and MyDSL_AlterformView.lua. Fixed by never locking the container at all: exitLabel (close) and minimizeLabel (min/restore) are plain Geyser.Label children created unconditionally in the constructor, so hiding them directly removes the button clutter while leaving self.locked = false, keeping mouse resize/move fully functional. Also added an unconditional unlockContainer() call on every script reload (MoonWeather's init(), Alterform's boot()), not just first creation, since the container Geyser object survives reloads and the original bug's home (_buildUI()/ensureUI()) never re-runs for someone already stuck locked -- without this, reloading the fixed script wouldn't have unstuck an already-locked live window. Also, per Steven's separate screenshot-confirmed ask in the same message ("the actual weather line at the top of moonweather needs to be on font size smaller to match the info below"): MoonWeather's weather row was left at 9pt from the earlier +1pt bump (same as the time row), one size bigger than the focal/bonus row's 8pt directly beneath it -- knocked back to 8pt to match, icon span 10pt→9pt to keep the same relative sizing. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed a second, separate real bug in the MoonWeather lunar countdown, per Steven after confirming the earlier mod-4 hours fix ("the regen cycle line timer is still not correct see the cycles. it will update correct then change to -1 the cycle reported"). MW.cyclesNow() computed elapsed_ticks as a raw fractional real number (e.g. 0.02 right after anchoring a fresh lunar read), then subtracted that directly from anchor_cycles -- so countdownStr()'s math.floor() on the result rounded a freshly-anchored, correct value like 33 down to 32 almost immediately, well before a full tick_avg-second interval had actually elapsed. Fixed by flooring elapsed_ticks itself before subtracting, so the displayed count now holds at the correct anchor value for the entire first interval and only steps down once a whole tick has genuinely completed. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: built RightHere's Known/Seen/Unknown mob tagging + name accuracy improvement, per Steven ("as we identify mobs, it should create a table to look up and find more accurate tags for targets... mob names are the most important... if we need to use an identifier alias, where we look then creaturelore, that could be an option"). Corpus research first: extracted 30 real look-vs-scan mob text pairs from the log/ archive and found the common case (plain unnamed mobs) already matches word-for-word after normalization, and that DSL's own target keyword matching only ever uses the last word of whatever name gets sent (commandArg(), MyDSL_TargetView.lua) -- so most surface wording differences don't actually change the command sent. Real divergence turned out narrower than assumed: named NPCs (which Steven then deprioritized -- "shopkeepers are not important") and generic/truncated look descriptions for mobs DSL never names specifically in room text (e.g. "A gnome is here using levers..." never says which gnome type). Searched sibling Mudlet profiles for prior art and found a real, working implementation of exactly this in the DSL1 profile's embedded current/*.xml (MyDSL.CreatureDB/MyDSL.TargetCompact): mobs are classified "known" (has real lore data)/"seen" (sighted via look or scan, never lored)/"unknown" (no record at all), derived from field presence rather than a separate flag, with colored badges in the mobs-here window. Adapted this directly onto our own already-existing, already-persistent, already-shared MyDSL_CreatureLore.lua DB instead of building a second table -- added CL.hasLore()/CL.knownState()/CL.markSeen(). MyDSL_DataLayer.lua's parseLookHereLine()/parseScanLine() now call markSeen() for every mob capture, and resolve to CreatureLore's stored name via the new resolveMobName() whenever that mob is "known" -- this is what actually improves targeting accuracy, since a known name came from a real successful lore cast rather than a possibly-truncated room description. Deliberately never guesses between multiple possible matches for a generic/truncated name (per Steven: "if we are unable to guess then a generic is better than none") -- falls back to the captured text unchanged whenever there's no confirmed "known" match, so this can only ever improve accuracy, never regress it. MyDSL_ScanView.lua's RightHere render now shows a colored [Known]/[Seen]/[Unknown] badge per mob line, same color scheme as the DSL1 reference (green/cyan/yellow). Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed PortraitView's image scaling, per Steven ("the portrait is supposed to shrink kien's .png to fit the window") after screenshots (taken while testing the RightHere tagging feature above) showed a tiny, blown-up corner of the character portrait instead of the whole picture shrunk to fit. Root cause: imageStyleFill()'s border-image: url(...) CSS paints into the border area (a 9-slice UI-frame technique meant for frame graphics), not the label's content area -- without matching border-image-slice/width values it never actually scaled the whole picture into the box, regardless of Mudlet version. That renderer was a deliberate workaround for a real Mudlet 4.20.1 issue (Label:setBackgroundImage() not reliably repainting inside a docked UserWindow), but "contain" mode was being silently coerced onto the same broken renderer either way, so it never had a chance to look right. Built a real contain-fit renderer (containImageHTML(), MyDSL_PortraitView.lua) using two genuine Mudlet/Geyser APIs -- getImageSize(path) for the file's native pixel dimensions, and label:get_width()/get_height() for the label's actual live rendered pixel size -- to compute a true aspect-preserving scale, then draws it via an <img> tag through label:echo(), the same mechanism MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua already uses successfully for its moon icons (a completely different Mudlet pathway from setBackgroundImage(), so unaffected by the original workaround's reasoning). Changed the default fit from "cover" to "contain" in both the in-code default and the already-persisted MyDSL/portraits/portrait_profiles.lua (which would otherwise have kept overriding the new default). cover/stretch still use the old, confirmed-unreliable border-image renderer -- not redesigned this pass, since Steven only asked about the shrink-to-fit case. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed the identical border-image bug in LocationView, per Steven ("should prob check location after targetview, since it has images too"). Confirmed via grep and MyDSL_LocationView.lua's own header comment ("Uses proven PortraitView/old CharPic render path: border-image... because setBackgroundImage()/contain is unreliable in Mudlet 4.20.1") that this file copied Portrait's broken renderer verbatim, so room pictures were never actually being scaled into the window either -- same root cause, same fix. Added containImageHTML() (identical approach to PortraitView.lua's function of the same name) using getImageSize()/label:get_width()/get_height(), drawn via an <img> tag through label:echo(). Changed the default fit from "cover" to "contain" in the in-code default, the loadProfiles() fallback (data.fit or M.config.fit or "cover" -> "contain"), and the already-persisted MyDSL/roompics/location_profiles.lua. cover/stretch still use the old renderer, unchanged. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed a real %-12s padding bug in TargetView's creaturelore stat block, found via screenshot ("Race: tinker gnomeLvl: 45", no space at all). %-12s only guarantees a minimum width -- "tinker gnome" is exactly 12 characters, so it got zero trailing padding and ran straight into "Lvl:". Widened to %-14s (MyDSL_TargetView.lua).
- 2026-07-12: found a likely systemic root cause behind several of today's window bugs -- docked UserWindows not getting their percentage-sized children recomputed, only floating/manually-resized ones do. Steven's screenshot sequence showed Tick rendering completely blank (or a near-zero-width sliver) both docked and freshly undocked-but-small, then rendering perfectly the instant it was manually dragged to a larger floating size, then apparently vanishing again after re-docking -- consistent with the Focus/TargetView button-grid bug found earlier the same day (coded 8%/8%/82% widths all rendering as ~equal thirds instead). Root cause candidate: Geyser.Container:reposition() (confirmed via Mudlet's own bundled GeyserContainer.lua) is what recomputes a window's own geometry and cascades that recompute down to every percentage-sized child -- its own doc comment says it's "called on window resize events." A real native drag-resize reliably fires that event; whatever event a UserWindow settling into its docked size fires apparently doesn't, at least not reliably under this Mudlet version, leaving every percentage-based child stuck with whatever geometry existed at creation time. Added one test call -- V.ui.win:reposition() inside MyDSL_TickView.lua's V.render(), which already fires once/sec off MyDSL.Timers.Slow -- as the smallest possible experiment to confirm the theory before touching every other window. If this fixes Tick, the real fix belongs in MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua (one place, applied to every managed window) instead of patching each module individually. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: reposition() test came back with no visible change (confirmed via Steven's report after reloading). Traced further and found the real root cause: a genuine Mudlet C++ bug, not ours. TMainConsole::getUserWindowSize() (added by upstream PR #9334, "Fix: crash when deleting and re-creating a user window," part of the exact 4.21/4.22 upgrade Steven just did) deliberately rejects a real, smaller-than-cached window size as "suspicious shrinkage" and returns the stale cached larger size instead, on the theory that a big shrink means the layout hasn't settled yet rather than "this window is just supposed to be small." Confirmed directly with a diagnostic added to MyDSL_TickView.lua's status command (mydsl tickview status): it reported win:get_width()=1187px while Tick's real docked slot was nowhere near that wide -- exactly the poisoned-cache signature. This is also what the Focus/TargetView button-grid bug was (coded 8%/8%/82% widths rendering as ~equal thirds). Confirmed reposition() couldn't have helped here since it reads the same poisoned get_width()/get_height() internally. Steven then confirmed this survives a full Mudlet restart, which narrowed it further: the poison isn't purely in-memory (that would clear on restart), it's being re-applied by Mudlet's own native window-layout persistence on every launch. Traced via Mudlet's own C++ source (mudlet::saveWindowLayout()/loadWindowLayout()/saveFloatingDockGeometries()) to two files that live outside any profile folder entirely -- getMudletPath(mainDataItemPath, ...) resolves to Mudlet's shared config root, not profiles/DSL2/, so this is one layout state shared across every Mudlet profile on the machine: ~/.config/mudlet/windowLayout.dat (Qt's native dock-state blob, 6.6KB of real data) and ~/.config/mudlet/windowLayoutGeometry.dat (floating-window geometries, 4 bytes/empty at the time this was found). Per Steven's explicit choice, backed both up to .bak and deleted the originals so Mudlet rebuilds a fresh layout state with nothing poisoned to restore -- resets every profile's remembered window layout on next launch, cosmetic only, no character/game data touched. Not yet confirmed this actually clears the bug; MyDSL_TickView.lua's reposition() call and the mydsl tickview status size diagnostic are left in place until confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: resetting the global windowLayout.dat/windowLayoutGeometry.dat made things visibly worse, not better -- Steven reported the whole panel layout collapsed into a thin sliver docked on the right after a fresh launch with those files removed, instead of a clean default layout. Restored both from the .bak backup. Re-tested once more (delete again, rearrange manually with Mudlet fully closed first) per Steven's request, but he then decided this whole approach was not worth the continued risk to his layout and reverted Mudlet 4.22.0 -> 4.20.1 instead. Confirmed: "reverted to 4.20.1 and all works again." This closes the investigation -- the root cause (TMainConsole::getUserWindowSize()'s "reject suspicious shrinkage" cache, added by upstream PR #9334) is a genuine Mudlet 4.21/4.22 regression, not a MyDSL bug, and staying on 4.20.1 avoids it entirely. Removed the now-dead V.ui.win:reposition() test call and the temporary mydsl tickview status size diagnostic from MyDSL_TickView.lua -- both were mid-investigation experiments for a bug that no longer applies on 4.20.1. Recorded as a durable decision in docs/TODO.md's DECISIONS RECORDED section (staying on 4.20.1 deliberately) rather than left as an open punch-list item, since there's nothing further for MyDSL's own code to do here.
- 2026-07-12: back on 4.20.1, Steven tried the new "contain" image renderer live on Portrait and Location and didn't like the look ("i prefer the old images stretching to fill, not fond of this border lookin location and portrait") -- letterboxing shows empty bars when the image and window proportions don't match, which reads as an unwanted border. Built a second real renderer, stretchImageHTML() (MyDSL_PortraitView.lua, MyDSL_LocationView.lua), using the same reliable <img>-via-echo() mechanism as containImageHTML() but with no aspect-ratio math at all -- width/height are just the label's own live pixel size, so the image always fills the box exactly, distorting proportions if needed rather than letterboxing or cropping. Changed the default fit from "contain" to "stretch" in both modules' in-code defaults, MyDSL_LocationView.lua's loadProfiles() fallback, and both already-persisted settings files (MyDSL/portraits/portrait_profiles.lua, MyDSL/roompics/location_profiles.lua). contain is still available (and still correct) via "mydsl portrait fit contain"/"mydsl location fit contain" for anyone who wants it later; cover is untouched, still on the old broken border-image renderer. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed LiveView's room-title wrap, per Steven ("room title line wraps, it needs to not, and spread across the top row, thats why its so long"). The room-title Label was only width="80%" -- the remaining 20% of that row sat unused (nothing else shares y="2%") -- widened to 96%, matching the row below's/hRule's own established 2%-margin convention in this same file. splitRoomName()'s manual <br>-based line-break threshold (deliberately splitting any name over 34 characters onto a second line) raised to 60, since every real room name observed live this session (e.g. "A Path To The Mystic Crystal Fields", 36 chars) is comfortably under that with the wider row -- the split logic still exists as a genuine safety net for a name actually too long to fit, not the normal case anymore. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: tightened PlayersNear's name-to-room spacing, per Steven ("can we reduce the space between the players name and the room, make the text tighter together for a smaller window without losing the coloring of the players name?"). DSL's own body lines use wide column-padding between name and room (real text DSL sends, not fabricated) -- added routePlayersNearBodyLine() (MyDSL_DataLayer.lua), which splits each line into its two real pieces (name, room) and copies each separately via selectSection()/copy()/appendBuffer() (the same rich-text-preserving mechanism Route.to() already uses elsewhere), so each piece keeps its own original color exactly, then joins them with a small fixed 2-space gap instead of DSL's wide padding. Falls back to routing the line unchanged (old behavior) if the expected two-piece shape isn't found in a given line. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: populated the Bestiary from shatteredarchive.com, per Steven ("can you populate the bestiary from the website https://shatteredarchive.com/library/view-beastiary . there are items on the website and maps as well. we will try to scrape it for more info later"). Confirmed MyDSL_CreatureReference.lua's Bestiary window has no database of its own -- it's a pure viewer over MyDSL_CreatureLore.lua's CL.db, the same DB backing RightHere's Known/Seen/Unknown badges. Scraped the bestiary list page (1,888 creature entries, all on one page, no pagination -- name, level, race, alignment, immunities/resistances/vulnerabilities/affects, tactics, continent/area all present as data attributes on each .beast-item link; HP/damage aren't on this list page, only on each creature's own detail page). Parsed into MyDSL/bestiary_scrape_import.lua, a plain staging file (1,635 distinct creatures after dedup -- 253 duplicate keys across different areas, kept first occurrence). Confirmed via direct inspection that CL.db is persisted in Mudlet's real table.save() reference/pickle format, not a plain nested table, so did not hand-serialize scraped data directly into it -- that risked silently producing a file that looks right but fails to reload correctly via the real table.load(), corrupting real captured creature data. Instead added CL.importScraped(path) and a "mydsl creaturelore import" alias to MyDSL_CreatureLore.lua, which runs inside real Mudlet using the real CL.save(). Verified the merge logic with a dry-run simulation against synthetic existing records before touching anything real: never overwrites any existing field (a real in-game creaturelore capture always wins), only fills genuine gaps for creatures already known, and adds new entries wholesale. Per Steven's explicit choice, scraped-only creatures count as "known" (green badge) the same as personally-lored ones -- no changes needed to hasLore()/knownState(), since they already just check for stat-field presence regardless of source. - 2026-07-12: fixed the Bestiary window's show/hide, per Steven ("bestiary show doesnt work, no window i can see"). CR.show()/CR.hide()/the auto-show-on-lore-update path all called the raw Geyser window's own :show()/:hide() directly, never touching MyDSL.Windows.registry[CR_WIN].visible -- the exact same "own local visibility tracking, independent of WindowRegistry's real state" bug already found and fixed in MyDSL_MoonWeather.lua the day before, so whatever re-syncs windows to their registry-tracked state (e.g. on character-identified) kept finding visible stuck at its default false and hiding the window right back. hide() was worse: it looked up MyDSL.Windows.windows[CR_WIN], a table that's never defined anywhere in MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua (confirmed via grep -- referenced in two files, created in none), so it silently did nothing every time regardless. Delegated entirely to MyDSL.Windows.show()/hide() (MyDSL_CreatureReference.lua), same fix pattern as MoonWeather. Confirmed the identical dead MyDSL.Windows.windows reference also exists in MyDSL_PortraitView.lua but is harmless there -- every call site uses getWindowObject() or P.window, so the fallback always saves it -- not touched. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed a real bug in the PlayersNear spacing-tightening fix from earlier the same day, per Steven's live report ("all sorts of things are breaking, players near you has room names in it"). The first routePlayersNearBodyLine() (MyDSL_DataLayer.lua) called selectSection()/copy()/con:appendBuffer() TWICE per line (once for the name, once for the room) with a con:echo("  ") in between to join them onto one line. Confirmed via the Mudlet forums (search: "appendBuffer... cursor stays on line 1, this output call doesn't update the cursor position") that appendBuffer() always lands its copied text as its own new line in the destination console -- it can't be called twice to build up a single combined line, regardless of any echo() calls in between; every other Route.to()-based window (History/Combat/Scan/Group/RightHere) only ever calls copy()+appendBuffer() once per routed line for exactly this reason. That's why the screenshots showed the player's name and their room as two stacked lines with no visible gap between them instead of one tightened line. Fixed by tightening the gap in place on the source line first (selectSection() the whitespace-only gap substring, replace("  ") it -- real whitespace edited, name/room text and their coloring completely untouched), then doing the normal single-shot selectCurrentLine()/copy()/appendBuffer() via MyDSL.Route.players(nil), matching every other routed window's pattern. Not yet live-confirmed.
- 2026-07-12: fixed the Bestiary window not refreshing when switching targets, per Steven's live report in the same message ("the targetview bestiary stats updated from creaturelore but the bestiary window shows no health/mana. is creaturelore saving to the right place?"). Checked the DB question directly first: read MyDSL/creaturelore_db.lua's real content (Mudlet's pickle/reference table.save() format), found the "mage" record's fragment with hp=159, magic=133 matching exactly what Focus had displayed -- CreatureLore was saving to the right place the whole time. The real gap: MyDSL_CreatureReference.lua only ever redrew on the "MyDSL.creaturelore.updated" event, fired right after a fresh "creaturelore <name>" capture completes -- unlike Focus (MyDSL_TargetView.lua), which also redraws on "MyDSL.target.updated" and every MyDSL.Timers.Slow tick. So selecting a target that already had lore stored from an earlier capture/session correctly populated Focus but never told Bestiary to redraw at all, leaving it showing whatever (or nothing) it had last rendered. Added CR.onTargetUpdate() + a "MyDSL.target.updated" listener (MyDSL_CreatureReference.lua), calling CR.render(t.name) on a new target or CR.render(nil) on clear -- deliberately does not auto-show the window on a mere target switch (unlike the existing auto-show-on-fresh-lore behavior), only keeps its content in sync for whenever it's already open or shown later. Not yet live-confirmed.

- 2026-07-16: fixed a real bug in MyDSL_ItemReference.lua found live within minutes of Steven wiring it in -- "[ LUA ] - error in event handler for MyDSL.itemlore.updated: ...MyDSL_ItemReference.lua:37: attempt to call global 'trim' (a nil value)". Root cause: itemKey()'s normalization called a bare trim(), incorrectly assumed to be a shared global helper -- confirmed via grep that trim() is NOT shared anywhere in this profile, every single file that needs it (MyDSL_AffectsView.lua, MyDSL_DataLayer.lua, MyDSL_AlterformView.lua, etc., 10+ files) defines its own local copy, and this new file never did. Fixed by dropping the trim() call entirely rather than adding a redundant local copy -- confirmed MyDSL_CreatureReference.lua's own equivalent name-normalization (render()'s key computation) doesn't trim either, since every real caller (an alias's matches[2], or rec.name from a merge event) already passes an already-clean string. The error firing at all is a positive signal: it means MyDSL_ItemLore.lua/MyDSL_ItemReference.lua are both correctly wired (dofile() entries confirmed present and working) and the identify-capture-to-merge-to-emit pipeline worked end-to-end on the first real attempt -- only the display function's own bug crashed. Syntax-checked via luajit. Ready for a clean live re-test.
- 2026-07-16: fixed a real off-by-one bug in MyDSL_AffectsView.lua's applyLinks() -- Steven reported "top 2 affect lines not clickable to cast, 2nd line and below work." The window-scan loop started at index=1, a value ported verbatim from PNP's own make_links() (PNP files/DSL_PNP_Affects.lua) -- but PNP's renderer prints a leading blank line + header row before any real content, so PNP's row 0 is genuinely blank and index=1 harmlessly lands on the header. MyDSL's A.display() has no such header -- clearWin() is immediately followed by the first real affect row, so row 0 IS content -- so starting the scan at 1 skipped it entirely, and with the default 2-column layout that's exactly the first two affects. Fixed: index=0. Syntax-checked via luajit. Needs live confirmation.
- 2026-07-16: fixed Bloodbath chat never routing -- MyDSL_ChatTriggers.lua's pattern required "Bloodbath:" to start the line (^\a?Bloodbath: '), but confirmed via log corpus the real format is always "<Name> Bloodbath: 'message'" or "(Imm) <Name> Bloodbath: 'message'" -- name (with optional immortal prefix) always precedes the verb, same shape every other channel in this file already uses. Fixed to ^\a?(?:\(Imm\) )?[^']+ Bloodbath:[^']*', matching established style. Syntax-checked via luajit. Needs live confirmation.

Added `CA._aliases.setSpellUsage` (`^setspell$`) to route the bare case there too. Syntax-checked via luajit. Needs live confirmation.

- 2026-07-16: while checking Steven's ground-vs-inventory item-name mapping question, found and fixed a real, currently-live RightHere capture bug -- fourth confirmed instance of the same class fixed 2026-07-08/09 for other sentence templates. Traced a real room-look (`log/2026-07-16#17-23-54.html`) line by line through `beginLook()`'s actual catch-all logic: `"This studded mace looks particularly dangerous."` matches none of `isLookFixtureLine()`/`parseLookHereLine()`/`isUnparsedPresenceLine()` (all three require "A"/"An"/"The" or a lies/lying/left/floats keyword; this line starts with "This" and has none of those keywords), so it fell through to `endLook()`, which would have silently dropped every entry listed after it in that room -- including a war mage and 3 novice mages at the very end of the same listing. Fixed the same way the prior three rounds were: broadened `isUnparsedPresenceLine()` to also treat "This "-led lines as skippable, mirroring the existing "A"/"An"/"The" check. Verified via a structural test harness (`test/mudlet_mock.lua` + the real trigger chain) fed all 43 real lines from the log: capture stays active throughout, war mage + all 3 novice mages present in the final `rightHere` table. - 2026-07-17: caught a real, non-obvious bug while merging: `return EMCO` was valid as the last statement of EMCO's own standalone chunk, but errors ("'<eof>' expected near 'MyDSL'") once other code follows it in the same file after merging -- a genuine Lua rule (return must be the final statement of a chunk), not something either half of the merge got wrong on its own. Caught because a stricter syntax check was used after noticing the original check pattern (`luajit -e "loadfile(x)" && echo OK`) has a real blind spot: loadfile() returns `nil, err` on failure rather than throwing, so that exact check always printed "SYNTAX OK" regardless of whether the file actually parsed, unless the whole invocation itself crashed. Re-verified every .lua file in the profile with the corrected check (`local f, err = loadfile(x); if not f then print(err) end`) after finding this -- one unrelated pre-existing issue found (MyDSL/settings.lua, stale DSL1-era data missing the `return` prefix real table.save() output always has, zero current code references it, not touched, out of scope) -- everything else confirmed clean.
