  Module design reuses existing MyDSL infrastructure rather than porting AlexK's original 2015 driver's own room-capture trigger chain: mob recognition subscribes once via `MyDSL.on("scan", ...)` (the real Lua-callback half of `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s event bus, confirmed to fire on every room reprint including after movement, not just a manual `look` -- fed by the same "[Exits: " anchor AlexK's own capture used) and matches against `scan.rightHere[key].raw`'s exact captured line, so area-mob matching is a plain string-equality check with zero re-parsing. Kill keywords are single-word (`MyDSL_TargetView.lua`'s `commandArg()` precedent, confirmed live: DSL's own kill-target matching fails on multi-word args) -- carried as this file's own tiny copy rather than an export, to keep the "separate outside addon" boundary strict (no edits to a passive-observation core module for this addon's benefit). Navigate-to-area calls the real `DSL_Generic_Mapper.xml` fork's `map.speedwalk(roomID)` once a start room id is cached (opportunistically, the first time a `start <area>` confirms arrival via the same defensive `getPlayerRoom()`-first fallback chain `MyDSL_LocationView.lua`'s own `mapperRoomId()` uses), falling back to showing the area's human-readable description text for manual first-time navigation. Session state is a first-class `stopped`/`navigating`/`paused`/`active` machine: `start` always lands in `paused` rather than immediately fighting, so a player can cast buffs/eat stat food first (per Steven's ask) before the same `resume`/`pause` pair that also works mid-run. Kill-stealing/"they aren't here" now finishes remaining enabled mobs already found in the room before advancing, an improvement over AlexK's "abandon room and move on" default. Failsafe dead-man's-switch reuses `MyDSL_CharacterAssist.lua`'s `tempTimer`+`killTimer` pattern; a new HP%-threshold safety net (`MyDSL.on("char", ...)`, since `update("char", ...)` already calls `MyDSL.emit("char")` on every `gmcp.char_data` event) sits on top of, not instead of, DSL's own wimpy. Command surface is `mydsl leveling <verb>` end to end (start/resume/pause/stop/status/areas/area new|delete|info/scan/show|hide/import/hp/timeout/buff), dispatched through a single catch-all alias + Lua `_cmd()` router, the same pattern `MyDSL_LocationView.lua`'s `locationCommand()` already uses. Added one `MyDSL.Windows.registry` entry (`MyDSL_Leveling`, visible=false, same on-demand precedent as Bestiary/Help/Item Reference) and one `MyDSL.Help.modules` entry under a new "Automation / Assist" category (which a future `MyDSL_Questing.lua` will also join) -- both are the two small, already-approved edits to shared core files this addon needed. Explicitly does NOT gate combat advancement on `MyDSL.combat.died`'s payload (considered and rejected as unnecessarily fragile for a first version -- normalizing a kill-keyword abbreviation against a full display name is inherently approximate) -- blind-advance-on-XP (`"You receive N experience points."`, corpus-confirmed real and current) is kept as the sole, simple advance signal, matching AlexK's own original mechanism exactly, so this is a pure addition of safety nets on top rather than a change to the core advance logic. Verified via a new 21-assertion structural test harness (`test/test_leveling.lua`): seed import (all 39 areas, the duplicated-key fix, the cryfield rename, re-import non-destructiveness), mob show/hide/hide-all toggling, scan-event-driven mob recognition (a real mob line queues a kill command, a non-mob fixture line never does), kill-stealing correctly trying the next mob in the same room rather than abandoning it, the XP-gain trigger's kill/xp/pending-key bookkeeping, the failsafe timer (captured via a local `tempTimer` monkey-patch, since the shared mock's own stub discards its callback) actually stopping the session when it fires, and the HP safety net stopping below threshold, staying quiet above it, and doing nothing at all when disabled (threshold 0). All existing test suites re-run clean after this and the two WindowRegistry/Help edits. **Not part of `MyDSL_Full.mpackage`/`build_mydsl_package.py`** by design (a separate outside addon, per the automation exception) -- needs its own manual `dofile()` wiring before it can run at all, and nothing here is live-tested yet; see `docs/TODO.md`.
- 2026-07-19: real live bug found and fixed the moment Steven wired `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` in ("that script blanks my main window, i had to disable"). Root cause confirmed in `MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua`: its registry table was built as `MyDSL.Windows.registry = MyDSL.Windows.registry or {...}` -- an idiom that assumes the registry doesn't exist yet, which is only true on a genuinely fresh Mudlet start. Adding a new Script entry and saving mid-session reloads scripts inside the SAME persistent Lua state (documented elsewhere in this codebase, `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s SECTION 1 comment), so `MyDSL.Windows.registry` already existed in memory from before -- meaning the entire literal table, including the brand-new `MyDSL_Leveling` entry added earlier today, was silently skipped rather than merged in. `MyDSL.Windows.ensure("MyDSL_Leveling")` then correctly returned nil for an unknown window, but `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`'s `ensureUI()` passed that nil straight through as a `Geyser.MiniConsole`'s parent container -- Geyser attaches a parentless console to the main window itself, at the requested `100%x100%`, which is exactly what blanked the screen. Fixed on both sides, not just patched over the symptom: (1) `MyDSL_WindowRegistry.lua`'s registry construction now merges any key from a `DEFAULT_REGISTRY` literal into an already-existing registry table instead of skipping the whole thing on `or {}` -- a pre-existing entry's live `.obj`/`.created` state is left untouched, but any window key only present in a newer version of the file now gets added on the next reload with no full Mudlet restart required, protecting every future new window the same way, not just this one. (2) `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`'s `ensureUI()` now checks `Windows.ensure()`'s return value and bails out cleanly with a debug warning instead of ever creating a `Geyser.MiniConsole` with a nil parent, regardless of why `ensure()` failed -- a second, independent layer of protection against this exact failure mode recurring for this specific window. Verified via 2 new regression tests: `test/test_windowregistry_merge.lua` (a pre-existing entry's live state survives a reload untouched; a window key only present in a newer file gets added, not silently dropped; the newly-added entry has real defaults, not a stub) and a new assertion added to `test/test_leveling.lua` (`ensureUI()` creates zero `Geyser.MiniConsole` instances when the window registry is simulated as unavailable, monkey-patching `Geyser.MiniConsole.new` locally the same way the eq-of-others test spied on `setLink`). All 6 test suites re-run clean. Steven needs to re-enable the `MyDSL_Leveling` script and confirm the main window stays intact before any further live testing of the leveling loop itself.
- 2026-07-19: second real live bug found and fixed right after the window-blanking one above -- Steven re-enabled the script (main window stayed intact this time) but then reported "mydsl leveling import looks like it died or never triggered," no error, no output at all. Root cause: `L.importSeedAreas()`'s default path was built from `getMudletHomeDir()`, which always resolves to whichever profile is CURRENTLY RUNNING the script -- this addon deliberately runs cross-profile by design (its Script entry `dofile()`s an absolute path from the MyDSL play profile straight into the DSL2 git repo, so fixes land on the next reload with no reinstall step), so the seed file was only ever looked for inside the MyDSL profile's own `MyDSL/` folder -- confirmed via `ls` against the real live profile directory that no such file exists there, it was only ever placed in the DSL2 repo's own `MyDSL/` folder. Compounding it: the "not found" failure path only called `debugc()`, which writes to Mudlet's separate Errors/debug console (not necessarily open) -- so the command silently did nothing, with zero visible sign anything went wrong, matching Steven's exact description. Fixed: `importSeedAreas()` now tries a short candidate list in order -- an explicit path argument if given, the current profile's own `MyDSL/` folder (so a genuinely standalone install still works unmodified), then the known DSL2 repo copy this addon is actually deployed from -- and reports "seed file not found" (with the full candidate list) via a visible main-console message instead of only `debugc()`, matching the same "echo(), not just debugc()" reasoning `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s combat-log work already established elsewhere in this codebase. Verified via 1 new assertion added to `test/test_leveling.lua` (calling `importSeedAreas(nil)` with no explicit path still resolves all 39 areas via the fallback chain, not just the profile-local `dataDir()` path). All 6 test suites re-run clean. Steven needs to re-run `mydsl leveling import` to confirm this actually resolves live before testing the rest of the loop.
- 2026-07-20: third real live bug found and fixed, this time via direct log analysis rather than guessing -- Steven reported "it did not engage the enemies" after successfully importing, navigating to, and resuming the "philosophy" area (a sub-slice of gahboom). Read the actual live session transcript (`MyDSL/log/2026-07-20#15-18-50.html` in the MyDSL profile, plus the character-bound `MyDSL/logs/combat/Olyndros/`/`focus/Olyndros/` logs Steven pointed at) end to end for the `mydsl leveling resume` window: confirmed the run correctly walked all 12 steps of the area (`[MyDSL.Leveling] philosophy: pass complete. 0 killed, 0 xp this run.`) but never sent a single `kill` command despite every room clearly showing real, seed-enabled mobs -- e.g. "Philosophy Guild" printed `"(Golden Aura) A gnome student is here."` and `"(Golden Aura) A gnome philosophy instructor is here."` on repeat, both of which are real entries in `philosophy`'s own mob list. Root cause: `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s `scan.rightHere[key].raw` field is deliberately the UNSTRIPPED original captured line (kept for display/audit, confirmed by direct reading of `parseLookHereLine()`), but the mob-matching code in `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`'s `MyDSL.on("scan", ...)` listener compared it against the seed data's raw mob text with plain `==` -- and the seed text (transcribed from a 2024 forum post) has no aura tag at all, while DSL is apparently applying some active `"(Golden Aura)"` effect to every entity in this zone/session right now. A leading tag on either side (charmed, glowing, any aura) silently broke every single comparison, for every mob, in every room, the entire run -- not a partial/edge-case miss, a total one, which matches Steven's report exactly. Fixed: added `stripLeadingTags()` (the same leading-parenthetical-stripping loop `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s own `parseLookHereLine()` already uses, extracted out of `deriveLabel()` which used to inline it) and now compare `mobDef.raw` against `stripLeadingTags(entry.raw)` instead of the raw line verbatim; `mydsl leveling scan`'s own auto-fill/dedup logic got the identical fix (stores and dedupes against the stripped text too, so a mob scanned while a tag happens to be active doesn't get a tag baked permanently into its stored text). Verified via 2 new assertions in `test/test_leveling.lua`, replaying the exact real "(Golden Aura) A gnome student is here."/"(Golden Aura) A gnome philosophy instructor is here." lines from the actual transcript: a kill command now gets sent for the aura-tagged mob, and the aura-tagged mount (present in the room but not a mob this area's seed data lists) correctly still never queues. All 6 test suites re-run clean. This is exactly the class of shared-risk gap the plan's own "followers/line-spacing" note flagged as the most likely place a still-unseen edge case would surface under a leveling run's much higher room-look volume than normal play -- though the actual mechanism (a transient aura tag, not a follower/pet idle-phrasing variant) turned out to be different from what was anticipated, and the fix landed entirely inside `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`, not `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua` -- DataLayer's own capture was already correct throughout (it stores the raw line faithfully; nothing there needed to change). Steven needs to re-test a full run to confirm mobs actually get engaged now.
- 2026-07-20: fourth real live bug found and fixed, this time via direct log analysis after Steven's re-test ("check notes, check logs, check logs for missing combat info, there are some errors showing in the logs"). Read the actual live session transcript (`MyDSL/log/2026-07-20#16-03-01.html` in the MyDSL profile) for the second `mydsl leveling resume` attempt: confirmed the aura-tag fix above worked -- real combat engaged, with continuous genuine swing text against a "gnome philosophy instructor" from 16:06:08 onward -- but `[MyDSL.Leveling] Failsafe: no activity for 30s -- stopping.` fired at 16:06:35, only 27 seconds into a fight that was still clearly, actively ongoing (a swing had landed just 2 seconds before the failsafe message), stopping the whole leveling session mid-fight against a mob that simply took longer than 30 seconds to kill. Root cause: `L.armFailsafe()`/`L.resetFailsafe()` were only ever reset from the `xpGain` trigger (a full kill) -- there was never any reset tied to ordinary combat-round activity, despite this being the plan's own explicitly stated design from the start ("reset on any combat-round activity... as well as on kill-confirm, not just kill-confirm alone -- so a long fight against a tough mob doesn't false-trigger") -- it just never actually got implemented in the shipped code, a real gap between the written plan and what was built, not something that regressed. Fixed: added `L._handlers.combatActivity = registerAnonymousEventHandler("MyDSL.combat.updated", function() L.resetFailsafe() end)` -- confirmed `MyDSL.combat.updated` is real (`MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s `combatRoundFlush` handler, `raiseEvent("MyDSL.combat.updated", rd)`, fires on every round flush) and, per earlier research this session, NOT on the `MyDSL.on()`/`emit()` Lua-callback bus (that's `char`/`scan`/`creaturelore` only) -- so this needed `registerAnonymousEventHandler`, the same mechanism `MyDSL_TargetView.lua`'s own `"MyDSL.combat.died"` listener uses, not `MyDSL.on()`. Verified via a new structural assertion in `test/test_leveling.lua` (the handler is registered at boot) -- the shared mock's `registerAnonymousEventHandler()` doesn't retain or invoke the passed function at all (a pre-existing test-harness limitation, not introduced by this fix), so full dispatch behavior can't be exercised without a live game; the fix's correctness rests on direct code review plus the real event's confirmed existence, not an executable test of the reset actually firing. **Separately investigated and flagged, but explicitly NOT touched**: the same session log showed repeated `[  LUA  ] - object: <Charge> function:<Trigger178>` / `attempt to index global 'dslpnp' (a nil value)` errors. Traced to a native Mudlet `Trigger` named "Charge" (`if dslpnp.battle.Active then ...`, sitting in `current/*.xml` since at least 2026-07-17, well before this addon existed) -- fires whenever Steven's own "charge" combat skill triggers, referencing the old PNP framework's `dslpnp` global, which isn't loaded in this profile. Confirmed entirely unrelated to `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` (no `dslpnp` reference anywhere in that file) -- a real, pre-existing, separate bug, left alone rather than touched without being asked, since fixing a native trigger not tracked as a git-managed file is a different class of change than anything else done this session. All 6 test suites re-run clean (`test/test_leveling.lua` now 27 assertions). Also consolidated `docs/TODO.md`'s `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` entry, which had grown to 4 separately-appended bug-fix paragraphs across this investigation, back into one clean, current punch-list entry -- per this project's own stated rule ("if this file is turning back into a growing append-only history again, prune it"), matching what `CHANGELOG.md` (this file, append-only by design) already records permanently. Steven needs to re-test a full run, ideally against a tougher/longer mob, to confirm the failsafe no longer fires mid-fight.
- 2026-07-20: fifth real bug found, and a substantial design simplification, both per Steven's direct feedback ("are you checking the game notes in mydsl? check the logs, this is definetly not working as intended, its to many steps to start, and combat and walking is inconsistent, whatever timer stops combat is not useful, just keep walking and fighting till you get back to the start point and give report like in PNP, we only need the pause resume and stop, not a fallback safety timer or whatever it is. see the notes, combat remains in the main window, the readicates and others should be going to combat with the condenser"). Checked the MyDSL profile's OWN `notes_utf8.txt` (separate from DSL2's, per his "are you checking the game notes in mydsl?" prompt -- confirmed a real file existed there that hadn't been checked before) for additional context: "mydsl leveling stopped without completion", "check olyndros logs, combat is spilling into main window", "mydsl leveling areas needs a cleaner display, it is very spaced out and doesnt need the [MyDSL.Leveing] line start".
  **Bug #5, the big one**: re-read the same live session log and found `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s core `combatDamage` capture trigger -- not Leveling-specific, affects every player's combat display -- never matched DSL's actual current combat text AT ALL. Confirmed via direct PCRE-equivalent testing (Python `re`, near-identical semantics to Mudlet's Qt-based regex engine for the constructs used here) against the real corpus: the trigger's final group required the line to end in one or more literal `.`/`!` characters, but every real swing DSL prints now ends in a parenthesized (possibly decimal) damage number instead -- `"Your wrath do UNSPEAKABLE things to Tinker gnome janitor (340)"`, `"Beautiful white charger's bite wounds Tinker gnome janitor (14.5)"` (pet/mount-assisted), `"Tinker gnome janitor's misses You (0)"` (third-party miss) -- not one of these matched the old pattern. Since nothing matched, `parseCombatDamageLine()` never ran for ordinary swings: no `round_data` accumulation, no Combat-window condenser summary, no gagging -- every real swing just printed raw and untracked straight to the main console, exactly matching "combat remains in the main window, the readicates and others should be going to combat with the condenser" and "check logs for missing combat info". Fixed: the target-name capture is now non-greedy and the final group accepts EITHER a parenthesized number OR the old literal punctuation, verified against all 9 real corpus line shapes (including the charge-skill's own literal-`!`-ending form, confirmed still works, not a regression). `parseCombatDamageLine()`'s own `punct` parameter only ever gets used as cosmetic trailing decoration on the raw per-swing display line -- the actual severity score always comes from a fixed per-verb `DAM_INFO[verb].score` lookup, never parsed from this text -- so passing a `"(340)"`-shaped punct through is safe. Verified via a new test file (`test/test_combat_damage_regex.lua`, 7 assertions) exercising `parseCombatDamageLine()` directly with the real corpus argument shapes (Lua has no PCRE engine to test the trigger regex itself against, matching this codebase's existing testing convention for trigger patterns -- regex correctness lives in the direct-testing methodology, downstream parsing logic is what the Lua suite covers). Investigated the separately-flagged `dslpnp`/"Charge" trigger errors from the same log too: confirmed unrelated, a pre-existing native Trigger (not a git-tracked file, not touched) referencing the old PNP framework's global -- Steven has since minimized it himself.
  **Design simplification**, once mobs were actually engaging (after the aura-tag fix) and combat was actually visible (after bug #5's fix), Steven's own direct experience playtesting drove 5 real behavior changes to `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`, all implemented same-session: (1) the failsafe timer -- previously just buggy (bug #4, prior entry) -- removed ENTIRELY, not patched further, per "whatever timer stops combat is not useful... we only need the pause resume and stop, not a fallback safety timer or whatever it is"; the separate, non-timer HP%-threshold safety net is an earlier, still-standing decision and was left in place, since Steven's complaint specifically named "timer" mechanisms. (2) A flee used to call `L.stop()` outright; now clears the stale room-mob queue/pending-kill state and calls `L.processStep()` to keep going instead, per "just keep walking and fighting till you get back to the start point" -- fleeing typically drops you in a random adjacent room, desyncing from the area's fixed dirs list, but per Steven's own explicit preference that's an accepted tradeoff (worst case a few harmless failed-move messages) over a hard stop on every flee. (3) The two-command start flow (`start <area>` to kick off navigation, then a SECOND `start <area>` call to confirm arrival before `resume` would unlock) collapsed to one command, per "its to many steps to start" -- `startArea()` now does its best-effort navigation (speedwalk if a room id is cached, else prints manual directions) and always lands directly in `paused`, no confirmation round-trip; room-id caching for next time's speedwalk moved to being opportunistic inside `resume()` instead of gating the whole flow. Removed `onArrivedAtStart()`/`confirmArrivalIfNavigating()` and the `sysSpeedwalkFinished` event-handler wiring entirely, not just bypassed. (4) New `L.report()` -- duration, kills, XP, XP/hr -- replaces the old terse one-line "pass complete. N killed, M xp" message, shown once when a full lap of the area's dirs list completes, per "give report like in PNP". (5) `mydsl leveling areas`/`area info` rebuilt to construct their whole table as one string and `cecho()` it once, instead of calling the `ce()` helper once per row -- `ce()` prepends a blank line AND the `"[MyDSL.Leveling]"` tag to every single call, so a ~39-row area listing was previously 39 blank-line-separated, individually re-tagged rows, exactly matching the MyDSL-profile note's "very spaced out... doesnt need the [MyDSL.Leveing] line start" complaint. All 5 changes verified via `test/test_leveling.lua`, now 33 assertions (up from 27): confirms `armFailsafe`/`resetFailsafe`/`clearFailsafe` no longer exist at all (not just unused), a flee leaves the session `active` and triggers a real subsequent movement command, `startArea()` lands directly in `paused` with the old confirm-arrival functions gone, the removed `timeout` command silently falls through to "unknown command" rather than writing to a now-nonexistent field, and completing a loop transitions to `stopped` with `L.report()` existing as a real callable. Also manually rendered `listAreas()`'s and `report()`'s actual output through the mock harness to eyeball the formatting directly (not just assert non-crash) -- both confirmed clean, compact, single-block output. All 7 test suites (`test_combat_damage_regex`, `test_itemlore_merge_fix`, `test_leveling`, `test_locationview_fixes`, `test_locationview_roompictures`, `test_others_equipment_hover`, `test_windowregistry_merge`) re-run clean. Consolidated `docs/TODO.md`'s `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` entry again (was heading back toward the same sprawl this same investigation already pruned once earlier today) into one current entry covering all 5 bugs and the design simplification together. Steven needs to re-test a full run -- the redesigned start→resume→walk-fight-loop→report flow, pause/resume mid-run, HP safety stop, and whether combat now actually shows up in the Combat window instead of the main console -- before any of this closes.

- 2026-07-21: sixth real live bug found and fixed, on the very next fresh profile load after the mpackage rebuild above -- Steven reported the exact Mudlet error banner verbatim: "Lua syntax error:...MyDSL_Leveling.lua:483: attempt to call field 'on' (a nil value)", noting it "has since been fixed, possibly by another script." Root cause: line 483 is `MyDSL.on("scan", function(scanState) ... end)` -- this requires `MyDSL.on` (defined by `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`) to already exist, but `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`'s Script entry deliberately lives OUTSIDE the `MyDSL_Full` package (per the addon-boundary design, `dofile()`'d straight from the DSL2 repo) -- so its position in the profile's overall Script execution order relative to `MyDSL_DataLayer` (embedded inside the package) isn't guaranteed, and evidently ran first this particular load. Confirmed this is a materially bigger problem than just the one failing line: a Lua runtime error aborts everything AFTER it in the same linear script execution too -- so this same failure also silently skipped alias registration (`L.makeAliases()`) and `L.boot()` itself for that entire load, not merely the `MyDSL.on("scan", ...)`/`MyDSL.on("char", ...)` calls -- Steven's own "has since been fixed, possibly by another script" phrasing describes Mudlet's generic error-banner behavior, not an actual guarantee that `MyDSL_Leveling` finished initializing correctly that session. Fixed with a new `onceDataLayerReady(fn)` helper (`MyDSL_Leveling.lua`) that calls `fn()` immediately if `MyDSL.on` already exists, otherwise retries via a short `tempTimer(0.5, ...)` poll until it does -- wraps both `MyDSL.on(...)` registrations (the scan-based mob-recognition listener, the HP safety-net listener) so a load-order race can never crash/abort the rest of this file's initialization again, regardless of where its Script entry happens to sit relative to the package on any given profile, including after future `MyDSL_Full` reinstalls that might reshuffle Script ordering. Verified via a new dedicated test (`test/test_leveling_load_order.lua`, 8 assertions) that deliberately `dofile()`s `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` BEFORE `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua` -- the exact real failure ordering -- confirming the whole file still loads with no error, aliases still get registered, `L.boot()` still completes (the areas table exists), a retry gets scheduled, and once `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua` subsequently loads and the retry fires, both deferred listeners end up genuinely registered in `MyDSL.listeners`. All 8 test suites (`test_combat_damage_regex`, `test_itemlore_merge_fix`, `test_leveling`, `test_leveling_load_order`, `test_locationview_fixes`, `test_locationview_roompictures`, `test_others_equipment_hover`, `test_windowregistry_merge`) re-run clean.

- 2026-07-21: seventh and eighth real live bugs found and fixed, per Steven's direct challenge ("check logs, there are issues, no combat, the path even seems incoroect? review code, review original leveling script and mydsl_full. why is it not working?"). Rather than re-guess from a surface log read, traced two real session logs LINE BY LINE against the actual capture-chain code. Bug #7, the real one: `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`'s `isUnparsedPresenceLine()` -- a fifth confirmed instance of a recurring bug class (2026-07-08 x2, 2026-07-09, 2026-07-16 were the first four) -- fails on `"     Several small desks are here positioned strategically."`, the literal first line of "Philosophy Guild"'s room content right after `[Exits: ...]`: plural ("are here", not "is here"), starts with neither an article nor "This", falls through every check in `beginLook()`'s catch-all straight to `endLook()`, silently ending capture before any of that room's real mobs (2 janitors, 4 students, 1 instructor) ever reach `scan.rightHere`. Confirmed directly against both real session logs: every visit where this line was present produced zero combat despite real, correctly-enabled, untagged mob text sitting right there in the same room listing; the one visit where the game happened not to print this particular furniture line produced real combat (`"Your thrust do UNSPEAKABLE things to Gnome philosophy instructor"`) within seconds. Before fixing, corpus-checked per this project's own "verify against source, don't trust summaries" standard: grepped the full DSL2 + MyDSL log corpus for every standalone line starting with "Several" -- 78 real occurrences, 7 distinct sentences, every single one furniture/scenery (booths, chairs, doors, tables, logs, wheelbarrows) -- zero real mob-describing counterexamples, meaning treating "Several " as a skippable-continuation token (mirroring "This "'s own 2026-07-16 precedent exactly) carries no confirmed risk of silently un-capturing a real mob group. This is a genuine `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua` fix, not a `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` one -- benefits every module reading `scan.rightHere`, exactly matching the shared-risk note that file's own header comment carried since day one ("a leveling run generates far more room-look volume than normal play, so it's the most likely place a still-unseen edge case would surface... fix it at the shared DataLayer level"). Verified via a new dedicated test (`test/test_datalayer_several_fixture_line.lua`, 4 assertions) replaying the exact real captured room content verbatim through the actual `beginLook()`/body-trigger/`endLook()` chain -- confirmed via `git stash` that all 4 assertions genuinely fail without the fix and pass with it, not just trivially true either way.
  Bug #8, found in the same pass: the same log showed `"(mapper): (error): No path to chosen room found."` right after a `"Navigating to philosophy (cached start room)..."` message -- `map.speedwalk()` fails by echoing to the map console itself, not by raising a Lua error, so `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`'s `pcall()` around it always reported success even when the speedwalk silently did nothing. The old `startArea()` only ever showed manual `area.description` directions in the "no cached room at all" branch -- a player whose cached-room speedwalk silently failed got zero fallback guidance, and the subsequent raw-dirs-list walk then started from wherever they actually stood instead of the intended room -- matching "the path even seems incorrect" directly (the dirs list itself was never wrong, it was just running from the wrong starting point with no way for the player to know). Fixed: `startArea()` now always echoes the manual directions as a fallback reference alongside a speedwalk attempt, not only when there's no cached room to try -- one harmless extra line when speedwalk actually works, a real fallback when it silently doesn't. Verified via a new assertion added to `test/test_leveling.lua` (now 35 assertions), simulating a present `_G.map.speedwalk` and confirming both the "Navigating..." and the fallback "Directions..." messages get echoed together.
  All 9 test suites (`test_combat_damage_regex`, `test_datalayer_several_fixture_line`, `test_itemlore_merge_fix`, `test_leveling`, `test_leveling_load_order`, `test_locationview_fixes`, `test_locationview_roompictures`, `test_others_equipment_hover`, `test_windowregistry_merge`) re-run clean. Bug #7 touches `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua`, which is embedded in the `MyDSL_Full` package (not `dofile()`'d live the way `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` is) -- needs another package rebuild + reinstall before Steven can retest, unlike the load-order fix immediately prior which took effect on a plain reload.

- 2026-07-21: full codebase audit + a real process change, per Steven ("is there a way to go back through all the decisions we made and things we learned about this code and compare it? we seem to be regressing to errors we 'fixed' and 'documented' long time ago like the </cyna> echos. would it improve if we used a newer ai agent?"). Traced the specific claim first: `</cyan>` used as a cecho closing tag (invalid -- Mudlet's real syntax is `<reset>`) was confirmed still live in `MyDSL_LiveView.lua:53` and `MyDSL_LocationView.lua:1246` (fixed immediately) -- but tracing CHANGELOG.md history showed this wasn't actually a *regression* of a documented fix; it was the same mistake independently made in different files at different times (a native `roller` trigger 2026-07-09, `MyDSL_CreatureReference.lua`'s hex-color tags 2026-07-11), with nobody ever sweeping the REST of the codebase once one instance was caught. `MyDSL_LiveView.lua`'s own instance dated back to the file's first commit (`07f0f45`) -- never fixed there at all, not a regression. Ran two background research/audit passes to act on this properly instead of just patching the two found instances:
  **Audit** (systematic re-check of every bug class with a real fix-history entry in `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, grepped against the current whole codebase): cecho closing tags now clean (only the 2 found instances existed). `table.load()` missing its required second argument -- clean, all 14 real call sites including newer files not in the original 2026-07-11 fix batch (`MyDSL_ItemLore.lua`, `MyDSL_Leveling.lua`) correctly pass both arguments. `table.unpack()` (doesn't exist in Mudlet's real Lua runtime) -- clean, zero occurrences. Duplicate native-trigger-vs-Lua-port races (the actual root cause of the 2026-07-09 roller bug) -- clean, no new overlaps found; the empty-script `"awful/excellent condition"` native triggers and the already-known/self-minimized `Charge`/`dslpnp` trigger are the only near-misses, both harmless. Event-name mismatches (raiseEvent/emit vs. registerAnonymousEventHandler/on) -- mostly clean (several that looked like mismatches route correctly through DataLayer's generic `update()` helper), but found one real dead orphaned event: `MyDSL.Live.ExitsColoredUpdated` (`MyDSL_LiveView.lua`), raised once with zero listeners anywhere -- removed. **The real find**: the room-look capture premature-termination bug class (`isUnparsedPresenceLine()`/`isLookFixtureLine()`/`beginLook()`'s catch-all -- fixed 5 times already before today, most recently for "Several small desks...") had 3 MORE confirmed live gaps, found by replicating the actual decision chain in Python and running it against the full log corpus (DSL2 + MyDSL, ~800 files): `"Sturdy barstools line the outside edge of the lengthy bar."` (confirmed dropping a real barmaid + a mount, `log/2026-06-30#21-23-23.txt:281`), `"(Glowing) High above the cityscape, a jagged rip mars the sky and crackles with charged energy."` (confirmed dropping an Elite Royal Guard + 2 good samaritans, same file:189), `"Dark marble benches are set facing the statue of the Sons of Liberty."` (ends capture on the literal first line after `[Exits:]`, `log/2026-06-30#20-29-23.txt:9666`). All three share the same shape as the 5 prior fixes -- a scenery sentence with no "here"/"in the room" anchor, but this time starting with unbounded adjectives ("Sturdy", "High", "Dark") that can't be generalized into a new leading-word allowlist entry the way "Several"/"This" could -- added as 3 new narrow literal substrings in `isLookFixtureLine()` instead, matching that function's own existing "floats above the ground" convention for exactly this situation. Writing the regression test surfaced a genuinely SEPARATE gap in the same investigation: the barmaid line ("A Dark Elven barmaid stands behind the bar, ready to take your order.") still failed to capture even once the fixture-line issue was fixed, because `parseLookHereLine()` had no pattern for "stands behind X"/"sits behind X" (only "stands/sits HERE") -- corpus-confirmed real and recurring (a gentleman, a young lady, Grokk, all "stands/sits behind the bar/counter/desk") -- added 2 new verb patterns. One assertion in the new test initially looked like a third bug (a mount "walks in" not being captured) but corpus-checking "Someone walks in." proved this is a transient arrival announcement (same mechanic as player movement messages), not room content -- correctly should NOT become a captured mob; fixed the test's own wrong expectation instead of the code. **Explicitly NOT decided unilaterally**: whether `beginLook()`'s whole catch-all should be redesigned from an ever-growing "recognize this specific continuation shape" allowlist to a "default to keep capturing unless a recognized terminator" model -- checked the corpus for this too and found real counterexamples (indented lines that ARE genuine mob-shaped presence lines, e.g. "A very large bind stone is here.", so blank/indentation-based heuristics aren't a safe blanket signal either) -- flagged as an open architectural question in `docs/TODO.md` for Steven's actual input rather than picked silently. Verified via 2 new test files (`test/test_datalayer_audit_fixture_lines.lua`, 5 assertions replaying the exact real corpus lines; `test/test_datalayer_several_fixture_line.lua` from the prior round still passing). All 10 test suites re-run clean.
  **Research** (how to catch this kind of thing proactively instead of waiting for the next live bug report, and whether a newer AI model would help): concluded no -- model capability isn't the bottleneck, systematic re-checking is. Found the real lever is Claude Code's own `PostToolUse` hook mechanism (official docs, `code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks`): a hook can run a shell command automatically after every `Edit`/`Write`, block with a reason fed straight back for immediate correction, configured in `.claude/settings.json` (project-level, shareable via git). `luacheck` (the standard Lua static analyzer) was investigated and rejected for this specific problem -- not installed in this environment, real setup cost (needs `luarocks` + a Lua toolchain bootstrap, plus a large custom globals-allowlist for Mudlet's own huge injected-global surface before it'd stop false-flagging every file), and more fundamentally the wrong shape: it parses Lua syntax, not the *contents* of strings, and the triggering bug lives entirely inside a cecho string -- structurally invisible to a generic linter without custom rules, at which point it's the same grep-based checker anyway, just inside luacheck's plugin system instead of a standalone script.
  **Adopted**: `scripts/check_known_patterns.py` -- a small script encoding real historical bugs (cecho closing tags, `table.load()` missing its second argument, `table.unpack()`, more as they come up) as grep rules, runnable two ways: as the `PostToolUse` hook (checks only the one file just touched, immediate), and as `--all` (checks every tracked `.lua` file, for an occasional full sweep that catches what's already latent in untouched files -- exactly what today's audit did by hand, now repeatable on demand). First version false-positived on the project's own historical comments describing past bugs in prose (e.g. "this used to call table.load(path) with no second argument") -- fixed by skipping full-line comments before pattern-matching. Verified both modes: a full sweep against the current repo reports clean; a simulated bad edit (`</cyan>` in a temp file) correctly produces a block decision with a specific, actionable reason; a correctly-formed edit produces no output. Wired into `.claude/settings.json` via a `PostToolUse` matcher on `Edit|Write`. `.gitignore` updated with a narrow exception (`.claude/*` + `!.claude/settings.json`) so the hook itself is git-tracked and survives across sessions/machines instead of living only on this one -- verified via `git add --dry-run` that the exception actually takes effect (`.claude/settings.local.json` and everything else under `.claude/` stays ignored, unchanged). `CLAUDE.md`'s Workflow section updated with the new habit: run `python3 scripts/check_known_patterns.py --all` periodically, and add a new rule here (not just a one-off grep) whenever a new bug class turns up that might exist elsewhere too.

- 2026-07-25: first genuinely complete MyDSL_Leveling.lua run, confirmed live -- Steven ran a real Olyndros leveling session on "philosophy" (per his direction: "performed leveling test with olyndros. see the note file for user and gameplay notes as well as olyndros log") and it completed a full lap with the new end-of-run report printing correctly: 17m11s duration, 8 killed, 1229 XP (4291/hr), plus two clean pause/resume cycles mid-run. First time the whole start->resume->walk-fight-loop->report flow has actually worked end to end, after 8 prior rounds of real bugs since 2026-07-19.
  Found and fixed 2 more real bugs from this same session, one from the log directly and one from Steven's own MyDSL-profile notes_utf8.txt: (1) Steven's note -- "damage still appearing in main window and not being moved to combat, eradicate/devastate/unspeakable etc." -- confirmed directly in the log: every single swing this session printed completely raw, untouched game text carrying real, varying damage numbers (297, 167.5, 173, 138.5...), which can only happen if MyDSL_DataLayer.lua's `combatDamage` trigger never matched the line at all (parseCombatDamageLine() always replaces a matched line with a FIXED per-verb-tier score from DAM_INFO, never the real number -- seeing the real number proves the trigger simply didn't fire). This is the exact same trigger fixed 2026-07-20 for the "(N)"-suffix damage format -- independently re-verified that fix's regex against the real captured corpus line via BOTH Python's `re` module AND real PCRE (`perl -e`, since Python's re isn't a perfect PCRE clone and Mudlet's own regex engine is genuinely PCRE-based) -- both matched correctly and extracted the right capture groups, confirming the pattern LOGIC is sound. Given no Lua error appears anywhere in the MyDSL profile's errors.txt for this session (ruling out a load-order/crash explanation), the remaining well-precedented suspect -- directly matching this exact file's own real bug history (the "[Exits: " leading-space anchor bug 2026-07-08, the indented-static-landmark-line anchor bug 2026-07-09) -- is invisible leading/trailing whitespace in DSL's real raw line that a plain-text HTML log capture doesn't visibly show. Hardened both the leading anchor (now tolerates `^\s*`) and the trailing anchor (now tolerates trailing `\s*` after the damage-number/punctuation group) as a defensive fix -- cheap and safe regardless of whether this turns out to be the actual cause. Explicitly flagged as NOT a confirmed root cause this time (unlike every other fix this session, which were verified against directly reproducible corpus evidence) -- if swings are still leaking to the main window after reinstalling, the established next step per this file's own history is a live debug trace (the technique that finally cracked the "[Exits: " self-retrigger bug back on 2026-07-08), since static analysis is now genuinely exhausted here.
  (2) Steven's note -- "target window not populating when im in combat, should become the target im fighting, have all the mob info etc." -- confirmed directly against this same session's log: zero Focus/TargetView activity anywhere in the whole run despite real, successful combat happening throughout. Root cause: MyDSL_Leveling.lua tracks its own kill target entirely independently (`L.session.pendingKillMobKey`) and never told the shared `MyDSL.Target` API (MyDSL_TargetView.lua) about it, so Focus never learned what was actually being fought. Fixed: `L.tryKill()` now calls `MyDSL.Target.set(mobDef.label, true, "leveling")` (guarded, `pcall`-wrapped, matching this file's established defensive-cross-module-call style) right when it sends a kill command -- Focus's own existing auto-clear/auto-advance-on-death logic (already keyed off the real `MyDSL.combat.died` event, built 2026-07-11 for manual target-clicking) should now handle clearing/advancing for free as Leveling kills mobs one after another, no separate wiring needed for that part.
  Verified via 2 new/expanded tests: a new assertion in `test/test_leveling.lua` confirming `tryKill()` calls `MyDSL.Target.set()` with the right name/is_mob/source, and confirming it still works fine (no crash) when `MyDSL.Target` doesn't exist at all -- had to carefully save/restore `L.session.mobsInRoom`/`pendingKillMobKey` around this new test block since an early draft accidentally consumed the shared mob queue the very next test section (kill-stealing) still depended on, causing a real, caught regression before it ever reached a commit. `test/test_leveling.lua` is now 37 assertions (up from 35). All 10 test suites plus a full `check_known_patterns.py --all` sweep re-run clean. Consolidated `docs/TODO.md`'s `MyDSL_Leveling.lua` entry again (10 real bugs' worth of history condensed into one current, scannable entry) rather than appending an 11th paragraph on top of an already-long entry. `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua` changed again in this round (the anchor hardening) -- needs another `MyDSL_Full.mpackage` rebuild + reinstall before Steven can confirm whether combat routing is actually fixed this time.

- 2026-07-25: **item 9 above ("combat leaking to the main window") turned out to be a false alarm, not a bug -- corrected same day after Steven kept re-testing and pasting exact examples (`"Your thrust MASSACRE Gnome student (50.5)"`, `"A gnome student has quite a few [50-74%]"`).** What looked like DSL's raw, uncaptured damage text leaking past the parser is actually MyDSL's OWN round-summary recap, working exactly as designed. Traced character-for-character to `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua:4584-4588` (`combatRoundFlush`, an anonymous handler on `"MyDSL.char.updated"`, fires once per round on GMCP `char_data`): `battleFormat(cfg.summary_format or "%a%r %n %v %t (%d)", {..., d = p.dam, v = calcDamVerb(p.dam, isYou), ...})` then `decho(str)` directly to the main console. `p.dam` is OUR OWN accumulated total for that attacker/target pair this round (sum of fixed `DAM_INFO` severity scores across every swing), not a real DSL damage roll; `calcDamVerb()` picks whichever tier-word's fixed score best fits that running total. This explains everything that looked like inconsistent regex behavior: the same displayed word (e.g. OBLITERATE) showed different numbers on different occasions because round totals genuinely vary swing to swing, while some single-hit rounds happened to show an exact `DAM_INFO` score (MASSACRE's `(50.5)` is exactly `DAM_INFO.MASSACRE.score`) -- a coincidence of that round having exactly one hit, not evidence either way about the regex. The accompanying condition line is the same story, one line over: `MyDSL_DataLayer.lua:3260` (`combat.pending_condition.screen = "<255,68,255>" .. name .. "<r> has " .. label .. " [" .. pct .. "]"`), decho'd immediately after the round summary at line 4594 -- matches Steven's pasted condition line exactly, word for word. Because `decho()` output is local script output rather than incoming game text, it was never reachable by any regex trigger -- not the real `combatDamage` trigger, and not a deliberately dumb, unanchored diagnostic trigger (`MyDSL_CombatDiag.lua`, a temporary throwaway script built this same investigation, never part of `MyDSL_Full`) built specifically to test whether Mudlet's trigger engine was seeing this text at all. It wasn't, for the correct reason: the diagnostic caught hundreds of genuinely incoming swing lines throughout the same fight, but zero of these recap/condition lines, which is exactly what "this text never arrives over the wire" predicts. Cross-checked against `MyDSL/logs/combat/<CharName>/<date>.log` (`MyDSL.logWindow("combat", ...)`, only ever written once a swing is actually parsed) for the same live session and confirmed individual swings were being captured correctly the entire time, including the exact moments Steven's flagged lines appeared -- the parser was never broken. The 2026-07-25 anchor-hardening regex change (item 9's original fix attempt) is harmless and doesn't need reverting, it just wasn't fixing anything real. Steven confirmed 2026-07-25 he wants the recap kept exactly as it is -- no code change needed. `MyDSL_CombatDiag.lua` is dead weight now; delete the file and its Script Editor entry.

- 2026-08-23: fixed a real LocationView bug, per Steven's MyDSL notes report ("mydsl location set 'A Sloped Hall.png' ... it looks like it set it, but it is not displaying"). Root cause: `mydsl location`'s alias captures its argument verbatim with no shell-style quote stripping anywhere in the dispatch chain -- Steven's typed command quoted the filename, and the real file (confirmed to genuinely exist on disk in the live MyDSL profile's roompics/ dir) never matched the constructed path once literal quote characters got baked into it, so M.render()'s exists() check always failed silently. M.setImage() itself had already "succeeded" and echoed a confirmation regardless, since writing the path into M.roomPictures never checks the file exists -- explaining exactly the "looks like it set it, but not displaying" symptom. Fixed with a new stripQuotes() helper in MyDSL_LocationView.lua, applied to both M.resolveImageInput() (the `set` path) and M.mapRoom()'s path argument (same bug class, not separately reported but sharing the identical root cause). Verified via 6 new assertions in test/test_locationview_roompictures.lua; confirmed via git stash that all 5 quote-related ones genuinely fail without the fix. All 10 test suites + check_known_patterns.py --all re-run clean.

- 2026-08-24: fixed the ItemLore third-party-identify capture gap, per Steven's MyDSL notes report ("if someone posts an identified item, the item reference captures that for its info, but its enchanted and not the normal stats, need a way to seperate or just not replace the info unless self identified"). Confirmed real via 3 distinct corpus-verified mechanisms all producing the exact same "Object '<name>' is type ..." line MyDSL.beginIdentify() fires on with zero way to tell them apart: a real self-cast identify ("c ident <target>", corpus-confirmed), a shop `insp`/`inspect <item>` command (confirmed via DSL_Helpfiles to show a shopkeeper's for-sale item, not the player's own possession), and `anote read` (confirmed via log/2026-07-04#12-43-48.html: a bulletin-board note whose body text quoted an identify-shaped block a seller pasted into their own auction note). Fixed with a sysDataSendRequest-based arming check (same technique DSL_Generic_Mapper.xml already uses for move-cost capture, plus the same 6-second freshness-window precedent as that file's DSL_CONTEXT_TIMEOUT) -- only genuinely self-cast identify commands tag the capture source="identify"; everything else gets tagged source="observed" and automatically receives the same safe fill-gaps-only treatment IL.merge() already gives `lore`, reusing the existing two-tier trust model instead of inventing a third. Caught a real ordering bug while writing this: the new isIdentifyCastCommand() local helper was initially placed AFTER beginIdentify() in the file, which would have made it invisible to beginIdentify()'s own lexical scope (a local declared later in a chunk isn't visible to code compiled earlier in that same chunk) -- moved before beginIdentify() instead of after, same class of mistake this file's own SECTION 1 header comment already warns about for _aliases/_triggers. New test/test_identify_source_scoping.lua, 5 assertions, confirmed via git stash that 4 genuinely fail without the fix. All 12 test suites + check_known_patterns.py --all re-run clean.

- 2026-08-24: built the modifier-less Song:/Spell: capture, a real gap found during the 2026-08-23 cross-profile log-corpus confirmation pass. "Song : song of war" / "Spell: toughness" (no "modifies ... by ... for ... cycles" clause) matched neither A.ids.triggers.song nor .spell in MyDSL_AffectsView.lua, both of which required that clause. Added .songBare/.spellBare using a negative-lookahead exclusion (`(?!.*modifies)`) so they never double-fire against the full-clause lines -- cross-checked this specific PCRE construct against both Python's re AND real PCRE via `perl -e` before shipping it, per the project's own newly-documented regex-verification standard (Lua patterns have no lookahead at all, so this is exactly the class of PCRE-specific behavior a near-equivalent engine could get wrong). New A.captureSpellLineBare(name) adds the affect with duration=-1 (no fabricated timer, matching A.add()'s own "unknown expiry" convention) and no modifier data. Likely the same real mechanism as a separate note already sitting in MyDSL's own notes_utf8.txt ("low level charcaters cant see timers for affects") -- not confirmed as literally the same trigger, but close enough to flag for live confirmation. 7 new test assertions in test/test_affects_bare_spell_song.lua; confirmed via git stash the fix is real (a nil-function-call crash without it, not just an assertion failure). All 13 test suites + check_known_patterns.py --all re-run clean.

- 2026-08-24: fixed MyDSL.logWindow()'s disabled-categories drift, per Steven's still-open ask ("stop logging anything except combat/main window/chat and history... others dont seem needed"). A per-category toggle already existed (built 2026-07-05/07-07 for exactly this ask) and combat/chat/history were already correctly on-by-default. But grepping every real MyDSL.logWindow() call site across the whole codebase found the disabled-categories list had drifted: `target`/`scan`/`bloodbath` were dead entries (no current code produces those category names -- leftovers from the old MyDSL.Route.scan()/combat()/group()/righthere() shorthands removed 2026-08-23), while `focus` (MyDSL_TargetView.lua's real, active category) was missing entirely -- so Focus/Target updates had been logging by default the whole time, contradicting Steven's stated wish, because the category was renamed from `target` to `focus` at some point without this list being updated. Removed the 3 dead entries, added the 1 real missing one, and fixed the matching stale category list in MyDSL_Help.lua's own `mydsl log` description. 9 new test assertions in test/test_logwindow_categories.lua including a real behavioral check (a disabled category writes no file at all, an enabled one really does, checked via actual file IO against the mock's sandboxed home dir, not just reading the config table); confirmed via git stash that 5 genuinely fail without the fix. All 14 test suites + check_known_patterns.py --all re-run clean.

- 2026-08-24: documented the `mydsl tick` vs `mydsl tickview` command-prefix split in `mydsl help`, per Claude.ai's namespace-consistency finding. Took the safe option: MyDSL_Help.lua's "tick" entry summary now explains it in one line ("'tickview' = the window, 'tick' = the averaging engine underneath it"), kept short enough to still read cleanly in the compact module-list view where the same summary string also appears (an earlier, longer draft didn't fit that context well, revised before committing). - 2026-08-24: fixed a real mapper terrain/room-coloring corruption bug in `DSL_Generic_Mapper.xml`, per Steven's direct report clarified in free text (walking into an unmapped room can leave `map.currentRoom` stuck on the previous room, so that room's terrain/color silently changes to match wherever the player actually is). Root cause confirmed by reading both real call sites (`applyRoomMetadata()`, `onTerrainLine()`) -- neither had any check that `map.currentRoom` actually matched where the player was. Fix: new `map.dsl.roomLooksStale(rid)`, comparing a fresh GMCP room name (the same ground truth `beforeExits()` already trusts) against the candidate room's own stored name; both functions now skip writing anything when it disagrees. Separately added the "set once, then manual only" behavior Steven asked for, mirroring the existing `dsl.weight_source=="manual"` guard for room weight: new `dsl.terrain_locked` room userdata set on the first successful auto-write, checked before any later auto pass touches the room again; a new `rt`/`room terrain [v<room id>] <name>` alias (mirrors the existing `rw` alias exactly) lets Steven override a locked room's terrain by hand. Added real-behaving map/room API mocks to `test/mudlet_mock.lua` (previously had none) and a new `test/test_mapper_terrain_lock.lua` (24 assertions); confirmed via a targeted revert of just the new guard lines that 5 assertions genuinely fail without the fix. Checked whether Steven's separate "or color the room box borders instead" ask was buildable: found Mudlet's real `setRoomBorderColor()`/`getRoomBorderColor()`/`clearRoomBorderColor()` API (confirmed in Mudlet's own GitHub source, PR #8758) exists only on its unreleased `development` branch (merged 2026-01-12, no milestone) -- absent from every shipped release through the current 4.22.0 stable and its 4.22.0 PTB betas, so it's not available in this profile's Mudlet 4.20.1 or any newer released version; deferred border color until Mudlet actually ships it rather than building against an API that doesn't exist yet. All 15 test suites + check_known_patterns.py --all re-run clean.

- 2026-08-24: verified `roomLooksStale()`'s core assumption against the real log/ corpus, per Claude Desktop's review pushback on 7a12a1d (that rd.room/GMCP and getRoomName()/text-scrape are two independent pipelines with no confirmed agreement, and a real formatting difference would silently stop coloring every affected room -- worse than the bug being fixed). Claude Desktop's own clone can't check this (log/ is gitignored for size), so this needed doing from a session with real filesystem access to it. Checked all 260 log/ files containing a GMCP dump: 98 total `room_data.room` occurrences, 89 matched a standalone displayed header line in the same file exactly. Investigated all 9 that didn't rather than treating them as inconclusive: 8 are the literal string `"darkness"` (DSL's own GMCP placeholder for "too dark to see the room name," confirmed via the surrounding log text -- never an actual room's name) and 1 is a log file that started mid-visit, after the header had already printed in a prior session's log (a capture-window artifact, not a formatting disagreement). Zero real evidence of GMCP-vs-display name drift. Turned the "darkness" finding into a real fix rather than just a footnote: `roomLooksStale()` now explicitly treats `"darkness"` as "name unknown," not a mismatch -- the old behavior was accidentally safe (skip = no corruption) but also needlessly blocked sector/color writes on rooms only ever visited in the dark, when the corpus confirms `rd.sector` keeps reporting correctly even then. 2 new assertions in `test/test_mapper_terrain_lock.lua` (26 total now), confirmed both fail without the darkness-handling line via a targeted revert. Reported the full verification back to Claude Desktop in `HANDOFF.md`, closing their specific ask. All 15 test suites + check_known_patterns.py --all re-run clean.
